## 0.69.0 - 2026-10-22
- Fixed the virtio 1.0 notification capability, which was read with the field offsets of the configuration capabilities. The multiplier, the offset and the bar of the notification area were therefore wrong, the device was never notified, and no transmit ever completed. The notification register of a queue now sits at the area base plus the offset plus the multiplier times the queue index, and the queue index is written there.
- The feature negotiation accepts the version 1 feature and nothing else. Accepting a feature the driver does not implement would let the device use a path that is not served here, such as a merged receive buffer, a flush or a discard.
- The queue size of a modern device is read back after the driver offers it, so the ring layout follows the size the device actually uses.
- The virtio drivers keep their state behind an interrupt safe lock and no longer hold it while waiting for a transmit to complete. The timer interrupt polls the network, so a plain spin lock deadlocked the guest as soon as a transmit was outstanding.
- Fixed the TCP send window: the acknowledged base of the window never moved, so the same bytes were retransmitted for ever and the connection was never closed. The close of a socket now completes.
- The user address space gives the program break and the dynamic interpreter a page table slot of their own, so a mapping in one region can no longer overwrite the page table entry of another.
- The SSE register file now survives an exception and an interrupt, so a faulting instruction that uses the vector registers is retried with the values the user program left there.
- A blocked fatal signal no longer ends the process that sent it, which is what Linux does.
- Removed a leftover debug print from the resource usage syscall. It wrote to the console while the process lock was held, which deadlocked the guest.

## 0.68.0 - 2026-10-21
- The running task identifier now comes from the scheduler instead of a cached copy, so a signal or a wait that names the current task cannot name a task that ran in between.

## 0.67.0 - 2026-10-21
- Fixed the user address layout: the program break, the anonymous mappings, the dynamic linker and the position independent image shared page table entries, so a mapping in one region overwrote the page table entry of another. The program break now lives in page table slot 131, the anonymous mappings in slot 130, the image in slot 129 and the linker in slot 128, and every region is alone in its slot.
- The loader accepts a dynamic image whose interpreter and main image live in different page table slots.
- This removes the intermittent memory corruption that broke the BusyBox and differential batteries: a program that used both the heap and an anonymous mapping lost the heap contents, and a C library allocator aborted on its own consistency check.

## 0.66.0 - 2026-10-21
- Added a virtio transport that speaks both device interfaces: the legacy port I/O device (version 0.9.5) and the modern memory mapped device (virtio 1.0). The modern interface is negotiated with the `VIRTIO_F_VERSION_1` feature and confirmed through the `FEATURES_OK` status bit.
- The virtio-block and virtio-net drivers now use that transport, so a default `virtio-blk-pci` or `virtio-net-pci` device model works. Before this the drivers only understood the legacy interface, so every device had to be started with `disable-modern=on`.
- The test harness no longer asks the emulator for a legacy device: the acceptance, differential and BusyBox batteries now run against the modern device models.
- Fixed the split virtqueue offsets: the available and used rings start with a flags word, so the index follows it.

## 0.65.0 - 2026-10-21
- Implemented the published x86-64 `rt_sigframe`: the restorer slot, the `ucontext_t` with the machine context in the published `REG_*` order and the `siginfo_t` with the sender in the `_kill` member. A handler with `SA_SIGINFO` now receives the signal number, the siginfo pointer and the ucontext pointer, like Linux.
- Replaced the `sysretq` syscall return with an `iretq` return: `SYSRET` loads RFLAGS from RAX, so the old path replaced the user flags with the syscall result. The user flags and the syscall result now both survive the call, and a signal handler is entered with the published argument registers.
- The delivered signal leaves the pending set, the pre-handler mask is restored on return, and `SA_RESETHAND`, `SA_NODEFER` and `SA_SIGINFO` behave like Linux.
- A blocking `wait4` or `waitid` reports `EINTR` for a signal with a handler, while a signal with the default "ignore" disposition (`SIGCHLD`) no longer interrupts the wait.
- A fatal signal that a process sends to itself ends the process before the syscall returns, but only when the default disposition is in effect.
- Raised the descriptor limits to the Linux values: 1024 descriptors per process and 512 open file descriptions, with a bitmap for the close-on-exec flags.
- The virtual-memory area ledger merges adjacent areas, reuses a released range and stores the inode number instead of a whole inode, so a process stays small and a long-running program no longer exhausts the table.
- `madvise` follows the Linux advice set (`MADV_DONTNEED` drops the pages, `MADV_FREE` keeps them, an unknown advice reports `EINVAL`) and `rseq` reports `ENOSYS` so a C library does not assume a registration that never happened.
- Added `tests/heap.c` and `tools/heap-test.sh`, a heap stress program that reproduces the allocator setup of a C library inside the guest.
- The firmware runner picks the first OVMF image pair that exists, so the acceptance gate works on distributions that only ship the 4 MiB build.

## 0.64.0 - 2026-10-20
- Made `mmap` with `MAP_FIXED` replace the previous mapping instead of failing with `EEXIST`, as Linux does; BusyBox's allocator relies on it to place a guard page below its heap.
- Made the program break grow repeatedly: the heap area is now replaced when it grows, so the second and later `brk` extensions succeed instead of failing with `EEXIST`.
- Fixed BusyBox's own allocator dying inside `malloc` after a failed heap setup; a sixty-command `sed` loop in the guest went from eight crashes to two.

## 0.63.0 - 2026-10-20
- Measured Linux compatibility with a real userspace: a static, non-PIE BusyBox 1.36.1 (musl) now runs in the guest and its output is compared byte for byte with the same binary on Linux (`tools/busybox-test.sh`, 115 checks over shell builtins, the filesystem, the text tools and process control).
- Fixed the syscall result being lost when a signal arrived: `rt_sigreturn` restores the interrupted register frame, but the dispatcher overwrote it, so `wait4` returned 0 and shells lost the exit status of their children.
- Path resolution now follows symlinks in every component, absolute link targets resolve from the root, and `readlinkat` reports the link itself instead of its target.
- Fixed short reads on regular files, which truncated every initramfs file to its first page.
- Implemented `rt_sigsuspend` properly (x86-64 passes the mask pointer in `rdi`; the old code stored the pointer as the mask) so shells waiting for children wake up, and added the missing `vfork` syscall.
- Fixed `execve` reusing the frames of the image it replaced (the teardown then freed the new image), and made `create` fail with `ENOENT` when the parent directory is missing.
- Made the initramfs files read-only so a write can no longer corrupt the kernel image, and made `fstat` report the live file size.
- Preserved the SSE register file across context switches (the sixteen XMM registers are saved per task) and enabled `CR4.OSFXSR`.

## 0.62.0 - 2026-10-19
- Relocated the kernel to a high-half image base at boot: a pre-relocation stage clones the firmware PML4, maps the image at `0xffff_9000_0000_0000` and a 1 GiB direct map at `0xffff_8000_0000_0000`, copies the loaded PE image, applies its `DIR64` base relocations, jumps high and drops every other PML4 slot.
- BootInfo ABI 2 reports the loader-chosen `image_base`/`image_size`, so self-relocation works regardless of where OVMF placed the image.
- Added a physical-access abstraction (`EarlyPhys` identity during table construction, direct-map `Identity` afterwards, `phys_to_virt`, page-walking `virt_to_phys`) and converted virtio-net/virtio-blk DMA, APIC/IOAPIC MMIO, initrd and VFS/process/seccomp frame access to it.
- Removed the `exec` slot-0 rejection: standard non-PIE static binaries linked at `0x400000` now run unmodified; user address-space clones keep the direct map and kernel image as supervisor mappings.
- Test stubs and `/init` now link at `0x400000`, and a `-no-pie` musl helper (`DIFF_NOPIE_OK`) verifies fork+exec of a low-address `ET_EXEC` binary.
- Fixed `virt_to_phys` to preserve the in-page offset, which had aliased DMA buffers within a page and broken ARP/network traffic.

## 0.61.0 - 2026-10-19
- Made pthreads work end to end under musl: `pthread_create`/`pthread_join`, contended mutexes, condition variables, `pthread_kill` and per-thread TLS.
- Implemented `CLONE_PARENT_SETTID`/`CLONE_CHILD_SETTID` tid writes (including correct `clone3` struct offsets), so musl's exiting threads release `__thread_list_lock` through the kernel's clear-child-tid write instead of deadlocking the first join.
- Added a per-task `tgid`: threads inherit their creator's group id, `gettid` returns the task id while `getpid` returns the group id, and `tkill`/`tgkill` deliver `pthread_kill` to the addressed thread.
- Fixed signal delivery from the syscall-exit path to use the live per-task stack frame, so a thread that blocked inside a syscall (for example in `nanosleep`) runs its handler instead of losing the signal against another task's stale frame.
- Stopped sending `SIGCHLD` for thread exits and switched futex waiters to `(uaddr, pid)` identity so woken waiters cannot lose their wakeup to a recycled wait-table slot.
- Reclaimed dead thread slots at the next thread spawn, folding each exited thread's CPU ticks into its thread-group leader like Linux does.
- Expanded native/guest differential tests with pthread create/join, condvar, pthread_kill and TLS cases.

## 0.60.0 - 2026-10-19
- Added per-process CPU-time accounting: the timer interrupt attributes each 1ms tick to user or kernel time from the interrupted CS.
- Implemented CPU clocks: `clock_gettime(CLOCK_PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID/CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID)` now return accounted CPU time and `clock_getres` reports the tick resolution.
- Implemented real `getrusage` (`RUSAGE_SELF`, `RUSAGE_THREAD`, `RUSAGE_CHILDREN`) and `times()`, and `wait4` now returns the reaped child's usage in `struct rusage`, folded into the parent's children totals.
- Made `ITIMER_VIRTUAL` and `ITIMER_PROF` functional: they count user (and kernel) ticks, fire `SIGVTALRM`/`SIGPROF` with interval reload, and `setitimer`/`getitimer` accept all three timers.
- `/proc/<pid>/stat` reports utime/stime/cutime/cstime and fixes the priority/nice/num_threads field positions.
- Expanded native/guest differential tests with CPU-clock, rusage and virtual-timer cases.

## 0.59.0 - 2026-10-19
- Implemented POSIX record locks: `fcntl` `F_GETLK`/`F_SETLK`/`F_SETLKW` with per-process ownership, range replacement with trimming and splitting, owner pid reporting, blocking waits that return `EINTR` on signals, release when any descriptor of the inode closes and at process exit.
- Implemented open-file-description locks `F_OFD_GETLK`/`F_OFD_SETLK`/`F_OFD_SETLKW`, owned by the description so `dup`/`fork` share them and the last close releases them.
- Added robust-futex death handling at task exit: the robust list is walked, still-owned words get `FUTEX_OWNER_DIED` with the TID cleared, and a waiter is woken when `FUTEX_WAITERS` was set, mirroring Linux.
- Fixed `MAP_SHARED|MAP_ANONYMOUS` mappings so forked processes keep sharing the same frames instead of copy-on-write splitting.
- Expanded native/guest differential tests with record-lock, OFD-lock and robust-futex cases.

## 0.58.0 - 2026-10-18
- Implemented the POSIX timer family (`timer_create/settime/gettime/getoverrun/delete`) with signal notification, `si_value`, overrun counting and `TIMER_ABSTIME`.
- Made `alarm`, `getitimer` and `setitimer` share a per-process `ITIMER_REAL` timer; `ITIMER_VIRTUAL`/`ITIMER_PROF` report `EINVAL` pending CPU-time accounting.
- Added `clock_settime` plus a CMOS RTC seed for `CLOCK_REALTIME`, truthful clock-id classification in `clock_gettime`/`clock_getres`, and `TIMER_ABSTIME` support in `clock_nanosleep`.
- Implemented `rt_sigpending`, `rt_sigtimedwait` with timer siginfo delivery, and `pause` returning `EINTR`.
- Added `set_robust_list`/`get_robust_list` storage and round trip.
- Fixed the timer IRQ stub to align the kernel stack dynamically before the Rust handler; a `movaps` in the new tick code exposed the statically assumed alignment.
- Expanded native/guest differential tests with timer, itimer, clock, robust-list and sigpending cases.

## 0.57.0 - 2026-10-18
- Added per-open-file status flags: `O_NONBLOCK` for opens, pipes and sockets, live-`O_APPEND` writes, and `FD_CLOEXEC` cleared on `execve`.
- Implemented the descriptor-control surface of `fcntl` (`F_DUPFD`, `F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC`, `F_GETFD`/`F_SETFD`, `F_GETFL`/`F_SETFL`, `F_GETLK`) and flag handling in `open`/`openat`, `socket`/`accept4`, and `dup3`.
- Added `flock(2)` advisory locks with blocking and `LOCK_NB` contention, wake-on-release, and per-open-file sharing.
- Routed `sendto`/`recvfrom`/`sendmsg`/`recvmsg` through `MSG_DONTWAIT`.
- Fixed a latent fork→exec→wait kernel stack overflow by doubling kernel stacks to 64 KiB and adding a syscall-entry stack guard.
- Expanded native/guest differential tests with flock contention, nonblocking pipe and fcntl flag round trips, append-after-lseek content, and close-on-exec across `execve`.

## 0.56.0 - 2026-10-18
- Replaced placeholder `/proc` data with truthful per-process cmdline, environ, status, stat, statm and maps, including `/proc/<pid>` introspection.
- Added dynamic `/proc/self/fd` enumeration and readlink targets for pipes, sockets, devices, inodes and anon inodes; generalized readlink/readlinkat over all proc links.
- Derived system files from live state: meminfo (frame allocator), uptime (monotonic clock), loadavg and mounts (scheduler and mount registry).
- Implemented `madvise(MADV_DONTNEED|MADV_FREE)` page discard, `MAP_POPULATE` eager fault-in, and truthful `mincore` residency reporting.
- Expanded native/guest differential tests with procfs truth, madvise zeroing and mincore/populate residency cases.

## 0.55.0 - 2026-10-18
- Added per-process descriptor, open-file, VM-area/page and scheduler-state accounting with exit-time reclamation checks.
- Moved mount routing into the VFS and added namespace-cloned tmpfs mounts with longest-prefix path selection.
- Increased recycled-frame capacity to cover the configured 256 MiB guest and fixed process-exit cleanup failures.
- Implemented capget/capset, capability-checked identity changes, `PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS`, and stricter seccomp filter validation and lifetime management.
- Hardened inotify watch matching, event records, overflow, close cleanup and create/modify delivery.
- Hardened io_uring parameter/ring bounds, descriptor lookup, read/write usercopy, and mapping lifetime; added native/guest NOP coverage.
- Expanded native/guest tests for errno behavior, `/proc/self/status`, capabilities, seccomp, inotify and io_uring; made the differential runner validate QEMU status and restore inputs on failure.

## 0.54.0 - 2026-10-18
- Expanded differential syscall coverage across file, directory, process, memory, time, identity and notification APIs.
- Added native versus guest execution comparison.
- Kept the full acceptance suite green.

## 0.53.0 - 2026-10-18
- Added a differential syscall test harness.
- Added tests for file, directory, process, stat, cwd and identity syscalls.
- Compared native and guest execution results.

## 0.52.0 - 2026-10-18
- Added per-namespace UTS hostname state.
- Added mount-namespace filtering for mount tables.
- Improved mount-aware path resolution.

## 0.51.0 - 2026-10-18
- Added per-namespace UTS hostname state.
- Improved sethostname propagation across namespaces.
- Delivered inotify events for filesystem changes.

## 0.50.0 - 2026-10-18
- Added read-only ext2 support on virtio-blk.
- Added /mnt integration and ext2 file reads.
- Parsed ext2 superblocks, group descriptors, inodes and directories.

## 0.49.0 - 2026-10-18

- Added synchronous NOP, read and write submission processing.
- Added completion queue entry posting.

## 0.48.0 - 2026-10-18

- Supported seccomp allow, errno, trap and kill actions.
- Validated and stored user-supplied filter programs.

## 0.47.0 - 2026-10-18

## 0.46.0 - 2026-10-18

## 0.45.0 - 2026-10-18

## 0.44.0 - 2026-10-18

## 0.43.0 - 2026-10-18

## 0.42.0 - 2026-10-18

## 0.41.0 - 2026-10-18

- Added block device VFS/file operations and seek support.

## 0.40.0 - 2026-10-18
- Added credentials, groups, capabilities, umask and namespace state to processes.
- Added setuid/setgid/setreuid/setregid/setresuid/setresgid/getgroups/setgroups/capget/capset/unshare/setns/seccomp syscalls.
- Added netlink sockets, inotify and signalfd support.
- Added a `/sys` virtual filesystem with kernel and device entries.

## 0.39.0 - 2026-10-18

- Added a minimal `/proc` filesystem with common self and system files.
- Added `timerfd_create`, `timerfd_settime`, and `timerfd_gettime`.
- Extended file descriptors with `TimerFd`.

## 0.38.0 - 2026-10-18

- Added compatibility syscalls/stubs: statfs, fstatfs, readlink, close_range, mincore, fallocate, sendfile, sync, getcpu, sched_setaffinity, mlock2, personality, nice, sync_file_range, xattr, fadvise, inotify/timerfd/signalfd stubs.
- Implemented `fallocate` growth and descriptor-to-descriptor `sendfile`.

## 0.37.0 - 2026-10-18
- Added cwd-aware path resolution across VFS syscalls and process state.
- Added filesystem syscalls: mkdir, rmdir, rename, chmod, fchmod, truncate, ftruncate, stat, lstat, unlink, dup.
- Added process/session syscalls: setpgid, getpgid, setsid, getsid, prctl, sysinfo.
- Added time/select/rlimit syscalls: gettimeofday, getrlimit, setrlimit, getrusage, times, select, pselect6, clock_getres, clock_nanosleep, utimensat.
- Added read-only file-backed mmap support.
- Extended libc wrappers and headers accordingly.

# Changelog

## 0.36.0 - 2026-10-18
- Added a CAM PCI scanner with multi-function and 64-bit BAR support plus x86-64 port-I/O primitives.
- Added a legacy virtio-net driver: split rings, RX recycling, synchronous TX completion, and the mandatory 10-byte legacy header.
- Added an Ethernet/ARP/IPv4/ICMP/UDP stack and a minimal TCP client (go-back-N, RTO, FIN shutdown) in `kernel/net.rs`.
- Wired `AF_INET` into the socket layer and Linux ABI: `socket`, `bind`, `connect`, `sendto`, `recvfrom`, `getsockname`, `getpeername`, `shutdown`, with `sockaddr_in` translation.
- Switched the acceptance guest to `virtio-net-pci` with QEMU user networking and added `tools/net-echo.py`, a dual UDP/TCP host echo server.
- Added `/bin/hello-net` (musl) exercising UDP and TCP echo round trips, chained after `/bin/hello-socket`.

## 0.28.0 - 2026-10-18
- Added a Linux signal ABI foundation: `kill`, `rt_sigaction`, `rt_sigprocmask` and `rt_sigreturn` syscalls in `kernel/signal.rs`.
- Added per-process pending and blocked signal masks to `Process`; `fork` preserves them, `execve` resets them.
- Added `libc/signal.c` with `kill`, `sigaction`, `sigprocmask` and `sigreturn` wrappers plus a `libc/include/signal.h` header.
- Added `signal_pending_check` before `SYSRET` in `arch/x86_64/syscall.S` and a `signal_deliver_asm` trampoline in `arch/x86_64/user.S`.
- Signal delivery is gated on a pending mask check; full handler-frame delivery lands in the next milestone.

## 0.27.0 - 2026-10-17
- Added a per-process address-space and descriptor owner (`kernel/process.rs`) with deep-copy `fork` of the private PML4 slots and child resume at the saved syscall return with `rax=0`.
- Added blocking pipes (`kernel/pipe.rs`) with 4 KiB ring buffers, EOF on last writer close and `EPIPE` on last reader close.
- Reworked `kernel/fd.rs` into reference-counted open-file descriptions with per-process descriptor tables shared correctly across `fork`, `dup2`/`dup3`, `pipe`/`pipe2` and a minimal `fcntl`.
- Added `clone` (fork subset), `wait4` with wait-status encoding, `WNOHANG`/`WUNTRACED`, process-group selectors and `ECHILD`.
- Added `ECHILD`, `EMFILE` and `EPIPE`; `sched_yield` now really yields; `execve` keeps descriptors open and the user stack grew to 8 pages.
- Added `libc/` `errno` support (`__errno_location`) and `fork`, `pipe`, `dup2`, `waitpid` wrappers; `/bin/hello-posix` exercises fork, pipe, dup2 and wait4 end to end.

## 0.26.0 - 2026-10-17
- Added a clean-room static C runtime under `libc/`: freestanding startup, Linux syscall shim, string functions, printf subset, first-fit malloc family, getenv and file I/O wrappers.
- Added `/bin/hello-c`, a C acceptance program compiled with Clang/LLD only and chained after `/bin/hello` through Linux `execve`.
- Extended the linker script with large-code-model input sections and the acceptance gate with C runtime markers.

## 0.2.0 - 2026-10-12
- Added versioned loader/kernel BootInfo ABI and memory-map records.
- Added bounded physical frame allocator with overflow checks.
- Added dependency-free spin lock.
- Added x86-64 MSR read/write primitives and syscall MSR constants.
- Replaced direct syscall pointer dereferences with chunked usercopy boundaries.
- Added early security invariants and milestone 2A roadmap entry.

## 0.3.0 - 2026-10-12
- Centralized Linux x86-64 syscall numbers and UAPI structure layouts.
- Added identity, PID/TID, close, fstat, brk, arch_prctl, set_tid_address, robust-list and prlimit bootstrap behavior.
- Added FS-base process state needed by libc thread-local storage setup.
- Added System V AMD64 auxiliary-vector builder for future ELF exec.
- Kept unsupported memory, signal, futex, filesystem and clock calls explicit as ENOSYS rather than faking success.

## 0.4.0 - 2026-10-12
- Replaced alignment-sensitive ELF casting with a bounds-checked byte parser.
- Added strict ELF64 PT_LOAD planning, overflow checks, interpreter detection and entry validation.
- Added a fixed-capacity userspace VMA ledger with placement, overlap checks, protection and removal.
- Added anonymous mmap, mprotect and munmap ABI paths without pretending hardware PTEs already exist.
- Added ENODEV and EEXIST errno values.

## 0.5.0 - 2026-10-12
- Added a complete allocation-free AMD64/Linux initial stack builder for argc, argv, envp and auxv.
- Added AT_RANDOM, AT_EXECFN and ELF metadata placement with bounds and NUL checks.
- Added a transaction-friendly ELF execution planner with page-aligned PT_LOAD mappings and a guarded user stack reservation.
- Added E2BIG and explicit limits for bootstrap argument and environment vectors.

## 0.6.0 - 2026-10-12
- Added a freestanding C/assembly UEFI application entry and BOOTX64.EFI build path.
- Added UEFI GetMemoryMap allocation, descriptor translation and bounded ExitBootServices retry loop.
- Connected validated BootInfo to the physical allocator and CPU descriptor initialization path.
- Added x86-64 four-level page-table map, unmap, protect, CR3 activation and INVLPG primitives.
- Added GDT, long-mode TSS, ring-0 stack, IST stack and IDT construction.
- Added fatal default, double-fault and page-fault assembly entry paths with CR2 diagnostics.

## 0.7.0 - 2026-10-12
- Added IA32_EFER/STAR/LSTAR/FMASK programming for 64-bit SYSCALL.
- Added assembly syscall entry, user-RSP capture, kernel-stack switch, register preservation and Rust dispatch.
- Added lower-canonical SYSRET RIP/RSP validation and fatal fallback.
- Added an iretq ring-3 entry trampoline with user selector and context validation.
- Added a no-libc static Linux acceptance ELF using only write and exit_group.

## 0.8.0 - 2026-10-12
- Added a COFF Rust-kernel build path and single-image BOOTX64.EFI link pipeline.
- Embedded the static Linux acceptance ELF into the UEFI image and exported it through BootInfo initrd fields.
- Added current-CR3 translation and bootstrap identity physical access.
- Added actual PT_LOAD frame allocation, user PTE installation, segment copying and BSS zeroing.
- Added a mapped one-page user stack, Linux initial stack copy, and automatic iretq entry.
- Made assembly/Rust boundaries explicitly SysV64 so the same kernel can be linked into a Microsoft-ABI UEFI image.

## 0.9.0 - 2026-10-12
- Replaced numeric-only user pointer checks with page-table-backed usercopy validation.
- Added effective USER/WRITABLE/PRESENT permission checks and page-boundary chunking.
- Added bounded physical-frame recycling with duplicate-free detection.
- Added transactional ELF page installation and rollback of PTEs and frames on failure.
- Removed unchecked byte writes from the ELF copy path.

## 0.10.0 - 2026-10-12
- Added a per-process bootstrap CR3 cloned from the live firmware root.
- Cleared dedicated PML4 slots before installing the Linux image and user stack.
- Moved the acceptance ELF to a separate 4 TiB PML4 slot.
- Activated the process CR3 immediately before the ring-3 IRET transition.
- Rejected ELF layouts that share the identity slot or cross the private image slot.

## 0.11.0 - 2026-10-12
- Added process address-space lifetime tracking across the CR3 switch.
- Added recursive teardown for private PML4 slots, including user frames and intermediate page tables.
- Restored the bootstrap CR3 before reclaiming the process root.
- Connected exit_group to address-space reclamation before CPU halt.
- Added a single-core QEMU/OVMF serial acceptance runner.

## 0.12.0 - 2026-10-12
- Added containment for user-mode CPU faults with process-only termination and address-space reclamation.
- Added explicit general-protection, page-fault and double-fault entry layouts.
- Recorded process exit status and terminating exception vector.
- Strengthened the Linux acceptance ELF to verify invalid write pointers return raw `-EFAULT`.
- Added distinct success and failure messages and nonzero failure exit status.

## 0.13.0 - 2026-10-12
- Corrected the Microsoft-x64 to SysV64 loader transition stack alignment.
- Added dedicated error-code exception stubs for invalid TSS, segment-not-present, stack-segment and alignment-check faults.
- Cleared DF before entering Rust exception handling.
- Added source consistency and host toolchain preflight scripts.
- Included compiler-builtins memory intrinsics in the cross-target Rust build.

## 0.14.0 - 2026-10-12

- Added a timeout-bounded acceptance harness that captures and validates serial output.
- Added automated checks for EFAULT success, zero exit status and full address-space reclamation.
- Updated the QEMU runner with the debug-exit device.

## 0.15.0 - 2026-10-12
- Added UART transmitter-ready polling instead of writing bytes blindly.
- Added CRLF normalization for serial terminals.
- Added bounded serial drain before QEMU debug-exit.
- Protected acceptance markers from being lost when the VM exits immediately after process teardown.

- Rejected CPUs that cannot support the required Linux x86-64 entry and NX model.
- Enabled CR0.WP so supervisor writes respect read-only PTEs.
- Added boot diagnostics for detected CPU protections.

## 0.17.0 - 2026-10-12
- Located ACPI RSDP through UEFI configuration tables and passed it through BootInfo.
- Added checksum-validated XSDT/RSDT and MADT discovery.
- Parsed Local APIC, IOAPIC, interrupt-source overrides and LAPIC address overrides.
- Initialized xAPIC, masked the legacy PIC and all IOAPIC redirection entries.
- Added a periodic Local APIC timer vector, full register-preserving IRQ entry and EOI processing.

- Added an interrupt-disabling spin lock for state shared with IRQ handlers.

- Added per-task 32 KiB kernel stacks and a SysV64 callee-state context switch.
- Added a round-robin runnable queue, cooperative yield, sleeping tasks and timer wakeups.

- Added kernel-thread creation and a two-thread context-switch self-test.

## 0.21.0 - 2026-10-12
- Replaced direct embedded-ELF boot with a read-only initramfs containing `/init` and `/etc/motd`.
- Added checksum-independent, bounds-checked NSAR initramfs parsing and absolute path lookup.
- Added read-only inode metadata and offset-based file reads.

- Connected anonymous private `mmap` to physical-frame allocation and user PTE creation.

- Connected `munmap` to PTE removal and physical-frame recycling.
- Connected upward `brk` growth to mapped read/write heap pages.

# Slop/MNU

**SLOP** means **Synthetic Low-quality Operating Platform**. **MNU** means **Maybe Not Unix**.

A clean-room, non-Linux, non-GNU x86-64 kernel aimed at Linux **userspace ABI** compatibility and source-level driver portability.

## Scope of this milestone

This archive is the large first engineering increment, not a claim of full Linux compatibility. It establishes:

- freestanding Rust kernel layout (`no_std`, no external crates)
- x86-64 entry code, serial console, panic path and halt loop
- Linux x86-64 syscall dispatcher with initial `write`, `getpid`, `uname`, `exit`, `exit_group`
- kernel-owned errno and UAPI types
- VFS, task, virtual-memory and device-driver contracts
- ELF64 type definitions and loader validation skeleton
- PCI device model and Linux-driver adaptation boundary
- architecture and clean-room licensing documents
- LLVM-only build path; no GCC, binutils, glibc, GNU make or Linux source

## Build prerequisites

- Rust nightly with `rust-src`
- LLVM `ld.lld`
- QEMU for execution (optional)

```sh
rustup component add rust-src
cargo +nightly build -Zbuild-std=core --release
./tools/link.sh
```

`tools/link.sh` emits `build/slop-mnu.elf`. This first milestone is entered by a Multiboot/UEFI-capable loader; replacing that temporary boot seam with the in-tree UEFI loader is milestone 2.

## License policy

Project-authored code is Apache-2.0. No Linux or GNU source is copied. Do not paste GPL code, generated headers derived from GPL headers, or Linux internal structure layouts. Public ABI facts must be recorded in `docs/compat-ledger.md` with provenance.

## Single-image UEFI acceptance build (v0.8)

```sh
rustup component add rust-src
./tools/build-uefi.sh
```

The output is `build/efi/EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI`. It contains the loader, no-std Rust kernel, privilege-transition assembly and the embedded static Linux acceptance ELF. This path currently assumes identity-accessible physical RAM in the firmware page tables and is intended for the first controlled QEMU/OVMF run.

## QEMU/OVMF acceptance run

```sh
OVMF_CODE=/path/to/OVMF_CODE.fd \
OVMF_VARS_TEMPLATE=/path/to/OVMF_VARS.fd \
./tools/run-qemu.sh
```

The runner uses one emulated CPU and serial output because the current syscall transition state is global rather than per-CPU. A successful run should print the Linux ELF message, report exit status 0, reclaim the private address space, and halt.

## Build preflight

```sh
./tools/check-toolchain.sh
```

This verifies the nightly Rust toolchain, Clang/LLD utilities, LLVM binary tools, Python, and critical source-level ABI connections before the UEFI build starts.

`./tools/check-source.sh` runs the source consistency gate and `tools/check-abi.py`, a compiler-free check of the published Linux layouts that the kernel and the in-tree libc publish. `./tools/make-snapshot.py` regenerates `SOURCE_SNAPSHOT.txt` from the working tree.

## Automated Linux ELF acceptance gate

```sh
OVMF_CODE=/path/to/OVMF_CODE.fd \
OVMF_VARS_TEMPLATE=/path/to/OVMF_VARS.fd \
./tools/acceptance-test.sh
```

The harness writes the complete guest console to `build/acceptance-serial.log` and prints `acceptance: PASS` only when the Linux ELF validates `-EFAULT`, exits with status 0, and its private address space is reclaimed.

## Read-only initramfs and VFS (v0.21)

The UEFI image embeds `build/initramfs.nsar`. At boot the kernel mounts it, resolves `/init` through VFS, and executes that regular file. The acceptance program also opens and reads `/etc/motd` through Linux `openat`, `read`, and `close` syscalls. NSAR is a temporary allocation-free bootstrap format, not a stable on-disk filesystem ABI.

## Hardware-backed anonymous memory (v0.22)

Anonymous private `mmap` and upward `brk` growth now allocate zeroed physical frames and install user PTEs. `mprotect` updates writable/NX permissions, while exact-range `munmap` removes PTEs and recycles frames. Partial VMA operations, demand paging, file-backed mappings, COW, and brk shrink remain future work.

## Static execve (v0.23)

`/init` now uses Linux `execve` to replace itself with `/bin/hello` from initramfs. Path, argv, and envp are copied from user memory before a new CR3 is built. The old image remains active on load failure; on success SYSRET is redirected to the new ELF entry and stack.

## Static C runtime (v0.26)

`/bin/hello-c` is the first C program built without any host libc. The in-tree `libc/` provides freestanding startup, a Linux syscall shim, string functions, a `printf` subset, a first-fit `malloc` family over anonymous mappings, `getenv` and `openat`/`read`/`close` wrappers. `/bin/hello` now hands the process to `/bin/hello-c` with `execve`, so the acceptance chain covers two address-space replacements and a C `main` returning 0. Build it standalone with `./tools/build-libc-test.sh`.

## Processes, pipes and wait4 (v0.27)

`/bin/hello-c` now execs `/bin/hello-posix`: a C program that creates a pipe, forks, has the child `dup2` the pipe write end onto stdout and exit with status 7, then reads the pipe to EOF, matches the message and reaps the child with `wait4`. Kernel-side this adds a per-process address-space and descriptor owner, deep-copy `fork` of the private PML4 slots, reference-counted open-file descriptions, blocking pipes with EOF/`EPIPE`, `clone`, `wait4`, `dup2`/`dup3`, `pipe`/`pipe2` and a minimal `fcntl`. The libc runtime now carries `errno` and `fork`/`pipe`/`dup2`/`waitpid` wrappers. Threads (`CLONE_VM`), copy-on-write and signals remain future work.

## Signal ABI foundation (v0.28)

The kernel accepts `kill`, `rt_sigaction`, `rt_sigprocmask` and `rt_sigreturn`. Per-process pending and blocked signal masks live in `kernel/signal.rs`; `Process` carries the mask fields so `fork` preserves them and `execve` resets them. `libc/signal.c` provides `kill`, `sigaction`, `sigprocmask` and `sigreturn` wrappers. `signal_pending_check` runs before `SYSRET`; full handler-frame delivery lands in the next milestone.

## Signal delivery and a correct syscall return (v0.29)

The syscall return path now uses `iretq` instead of `sysretq`. `SYSRET` loads RFLAGS from RAX, so the previous path replaced the user flags with the syscall result and faulted the user process; `iretq` restores RIP, CS, RFLAGS, RSP and SS and leaves the result in RAX.

Signals are delivered for real. The kernel builds the published x86-64 `rt_sigframe` on the user stack: the restorer address at the bottom, then the `ucontext_t` with the machine context in the published `REG_*` order, then the `siginfo_t`. The handler runs with the signal number, the siginfo and the ucontext in RDI, RSI and RDX, and `rt_sigreturn` restores the interrupted context, the flags and the signal mask. `SA_SIGINFO`, `SA_NODEFER`, `SA_RESETHAND` and the `SA_RESTORER` requirement are enforced, `kill` reaches the requested process or process group, a blocked signal stays pending, and a blocking wait reports `EINTR`. `/bin/hello-signal` covers the handler, the ignored signal, the blocked signal, the default action and the stop/continue path.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: README.md

FILE: arch/x86_64/context.S

SHA-256: 0087be549f8a4007ed82108ff45b350fb4f0f4afbc1bae5df240f17bdfbee0f8
================================================================================
.text
.global switch_context
#ifdef __ELF__
.type switch_context,@function
#endif
/* SysV64: RDI=&old_rsp, RSI=new_rsp, RDX=old_fpu_area, RCX=new_fpu_area.
   Saves callee-preserved state and the SSE register file, so code that uses
   XMM registers keeps its values across a context switch. The area holds
   the sixteen 16-byte registers; FXSAVE is avoided because it traps on
   several TCG builds. */
switch_context:
    push %rbp
    push %rbx
    push %r12
    push %r13
    push %r14
    push %r15
    mov %rsp,(%rdi)
    movups %xmm0,0(%rdx)
    movups %xmm1,16(%rdx)
    movups %xmm2,32(%rdx)
    movups %xmm3,48(%rdx)
    movups %xmm4,64(%rdx)
    movups %xmm5,80(%rdx)
    movups %xmm6,96(%rdx)
    movups %xmm7,112(%rdx)
    movups %xmm8,128(%rdx)
    movups %xmm9,144(%rdx)
    movups %xmm10,160(%rdx)
    movups %xmm11,176(%rdx)
    movups %xmm12,192(%rdx)
    movups %xmm13,208(%rdx)
    movups %xmm14,224(%rdx)
    movups %xmm15,240(%rdx)
    mov %rsi,%rsp
    movups 0(%rcx),%xmm0
    movups 16(%rcx),%xmm1
    movups 32(%rcx),%xmm2
    movups 48(%rcx),%xmm3
    movups 64(%rcx),%xmm4
    movups 80(%rcx),%xmm5
    movups 96(%rcx),%xmm6
    movups 112(%rcx),%xmm7
    movups 128(%rcx),%xmm8
    movups 144(%rcx),%xmm9
    movups 160(%rcx),%xmm10
    movups 176(%rcx),%xmm11
    movups 192(%rcx),%xmm12
    movups 208(%rcx),%xmm13
    movups 224(%rcx),%xmm14
    movups 240(%rcx),%xmm15
    pop %r15
    pop %r14
    pop %r13
    pop %r12
    pop %rbx
    pop %rbp
    ret
#ifdef __ELF__
.size switch_context,.-switch_context
#endif

.text
.global fpu_save_regs
#ifdef __ELF__
.type fpu_save_regs,@function
#endif
/* rdi = area; copies the current SSE register file into it. */
fpu_save_regs:
    movups %xmm0,0(%rdi)
    movups %xmm1,16(%rdi)
    movups %xmm2,32(%rdi)
    movups %xmm3,48(%rdi)
    movups %xmm4,64(%rdi)
    movups %xmm5,80(%rdi)
    movups %xmm6,96(%rdi)
    movups %xmm7,112(%rdi)
    movups %xmm8,128(%rdi)
    movups %xmm9,144(%rdi)
    movups %xmm10,160(%rdi)
    movups %xmm11,176(%rdi)
    movups %xmm12,192(%rdi)
    movups %xmm13,208(%rdi)
    movups %xmm14,224(%rdi)
    movups %xmm15,240(%rdi)
    ret
#ifdef __ELF__
.size fpu_save_regs,.-fpu_save_regs
#endif
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: arch/x86_64/context.S

.section .text.boot,"ax"
.global _start
.type _start,@function
_start:
    cli
    lea boot_stack_top(%rip), %rsp
    xor %rbp, %rbp
    mov %rax, %rdi
    mov %rbx, %rsi
    call kmain
1:  hlt
    jmp 1b
.section .bss.stack,"aw",@nobits
.align 16
boot_stack:
    .skip 65536
boot_stack_top:

FILE: arch/x86_64/exceptions.S
SIZE: 2980 bytes
SHA-256: 6bb9fd913b1a5907c6f506a0f8deb1ba160ace3eef7dca5e06b4f59a74f618f2
================================================================================
.text
.global isr_default,isr_invalid_tss,isr_segment_not_present,isr_stack_segment
.global isr_general_protection,isr_page_fault,isr_alignment_check,isr_double_fault
/* Exceptions without a CPU error code get a synthetic zero. */
isr_default: pushq $0; pushq $255; jmp common_exception
/* Exceptions below already have a CPU-pushed error code. */
isr_double_fault: pushq $8; jmp common_exception
isr_invalid_tss: pushq $10; jmp common_exception
isr_segment_not_present: pushq $11; jmp common_exception
isr_stack_segment: pushq $12; jmp common_exception
isr_general_protection: pushq $13; jmp common_exception
isr_page_fault: pushq $14; jmp common_exception
isr_alignment_check: pushq $17; jmp common_exception
/* The CPU saves rip/cs/rflags/rsp/ss on privilege transitions but not the
   general-purpose registers. Demand paging and COW resume user code through
   iretq after handling a fault, so every GPR the handler may clobber has to be
   saved here and restored before returning. */
common_exception:
    /* The faulting instruction is retried after the handler, so the SSE
       register file has to survive the call into Rust unchanged. The kernel
       itself uses XMM registers, and a demand-paging or COW fault on a
       `movups` would otherwise write the handler's leftovers back to user
       memory. */
    sub $256, %rsp
    movups %xmm0,0(%rsp)
    movups %xmm1,16(%rsp)
    movups %xmm2,32(%rsp)
    movups %xmm3,48(%rsp)
    movups %xmm4,64(%rsp)
    movups %xmm5,80(%rsp)
    movups %xmm6,96(%rsp)
    movups %xmm7,112(%rsp)
    movups %xmm8,128(%rsp)
    movups %xmm9,144(%rsp)
    movups %xmm10,160(%rsp)

.global apic_timer_entry
#ifdef __ELF__
.type apic_timer_entry,@function
#endif
apic_timer_entry:
    cld
    /* An interrupt can preempt user code and return to it, and it can also
       preempt kernel code in the middle of an SSE computation. Save the SSE
       register file around the handler; context switches save their own
       copy, but they cannot recover what the handler has already clobbered. */
    sub $256, %rsp
    movups %xmm0,0(%rsp)
    movups %xmm1,16(%rsp)
    movups %xmm2,32(%rsp)

    push %r11
    push %rbx

    push %r13
    push %r14

FILE: arch/x86_64/syscall.S
SIZE: 3700 bytes
SHA-256: e8fce1147115339757c6c7924ca324ccb7fb1b44696e25770ee27da4e078cba8
================================================================================
.text
.global syscall_entry
#ifdef __ELF__
.type syscall_entry,@function
#endif
syscall_entry:
    /* SYSCALL leaves user RIP in RCX and RFLAGS in R11; it does not switch RSP. */
    mov %rsp, SYSCALL_USER_RSP(%rip)
    mov SYSCALL_KERNEL_RSP(%rip), %rsp
    /* Linux guarantees every user register except RAX, RCX and R11 survives a syscall. */
    push %r11
    push %rcx
    push %rax
    push %rdi
    push %rsi
    push %rdx
    push %r8
    push %r9
    push %r10
    push %rbx
    push %rbp
    push %r12
    push %r13
    push %r14
    push %r15
    /* Frame: +0 r15 +8 r14 +16 r13 +24 r12 +32 rbp +40 rbx +48 r10 +56 r9
       +64 r8 +72 rdx +80 rsi +88 rdi +96 rax(nr) +104 rcx +112 r11.
       Linux ABI: rax=nr, rdi,rsi,rdx,r10,r8,r9=args. SysV call has arg7 on stack. */
    mov %rsp, SYSCALL_FRAME(%rip)
    mov 96(%rsp), %rdi
    mov 88(%rsp), %rsi
    mov 80(%rsp), %rdx
    mov 72(%rsp), %rcx
    mov 48(%rsp), %r8
    mov 64(%rsp), %r9
    push 56(%rsp)
    movb $0, SIGRET_VALID(%rip)
    call syscall_dispatch_raw
    add $8, %rsp
    /* The dispatcher result travels back to the user in RAX. The saved RAX
       slot keeps the syscall number, so an interrupted syscall can be
       restarted with the same number. The result is parked in a scratch
       cell because the signal check below is a call. */
    mov %rax, SYSCALL_RESULT(%rip)
    cmpb $0, EXEC_PENDING(%rip)
    je .Lcheck_signal
    /* An execve redirects the return to the new image. */
    mov EXEC_RIP(%rip), %rdx
    mov %rdx, 104(%rsp)
    movb $0, EXEC_PENDING(%rip)
.Lcheck_signal:
    /* A queued signal is delivered before the process returns to user mode.
       The kernel builds the published rt_sigframe on the user stack and
       rewrites the register frame so the handler is entered with the signal
       number, the siginfo pointer and the ucontext pointer. The frame lives
       on this task's kernel stack, so a task that blocked inside a syscall
       still finds its own context. */
    mov %rsp, %rdi
    /* The saved frame holds an odd number of words, so the stack is 8 mod 16
       here. The System V ABI wants 16-byte alignment at a call, so the
       alignment is restored before the call. */
    sub $8, %rsp
    call signal_pending_check
    add $8, %rsp
    /* The syscall result travels back to the user in RAX. A signal handler
       that returned through rt_sigreturn has its own value. */
    mov SYSCALL_RESULT(%rip), %rax
    cmpb $0, SIGRET_VALID(%rip)
    je .Lno_sigret
    mov SIGRET_VALUE(%rip), %rax
.Lno_sigret:
    /* SYSRET loads RFLAGS from RAX, so a SYSRET return would replace the user
       flags with the syscall result. The return uses IRETQ instead: it
       restores RIP, CS, RFLAGS, RSP and SS and leaves RAX alone, which keeps
       the syscall result and the user flags intact. */
    mov 104(%rsp), %rdx
    shr $47, %rdx
    jne .Lbad_pre
    mov SYSCALL_USER_RSP(%rip), %rdx
    shr $47, %rdx
    jne .Lbad_pre
    pop %r15
    pop %r14
    pop %r13
    pop %r12
    pop %rbp
    pop %rbx
    pop %r10
    pop %r9
    pop %r8
    pop %rdx
    pop %rsi
    pop %rdi
    /* The saved user RAX is dropped; RAX keeps the dispatcher result. */
    add $8, %rsp
    pop %rcx
    pop %r11
    /* IRETQ frame, pushed in reverse pop order: SS, RSP, RFLAGS, CS, RIP. */
    push USER_SS(%rip)
    push SYSCALL_USER_RSP(%rip)
    push %r11
    push USER_CS(%rip)
    push %rcx
    iretq
.Lbad_pre:
    mov 104(%rsp), %rcx
    mov SYSCALL_USER_RSP(%rip), %rdx
    jmp .Lbad
.Lbad:
    mov %rcx, %rdi
    mov %rdx, %rsi
    mov SYSCALL_KERNEL_RSP(%rip), %rsp
    and $-16, %rsp
    call bad_sysret
#ifdef __ELF__
.size syscall_entry,.-syscall_entry
#endif
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: arch/x86_64/syscall.S

.global enter_user_asm
#ifdef __ELF__
.type enter_user_asm,@function
#endif
enter_user_asm:
    cli
    mov %r8, %rax
    mov %ax, %ds
    mov %ax, %es
    push %r8
    push %rsi
    push %rdx
    push %rcx
    push %rdi
    iretq
#ifdef __ELF__
.size enter_user_asm,.-enter_user_asm
#endif

.global resume_user_asm
#ifdef __ELF__
.type resume_user_asm,@function
#endif
/* resume_user_asm(frame, user CS, user SS): restores a UserFrame snapshot and
   iretq's back to CPL3 with RAX set by the frame (0 for fork children). */
resume_user_asm:
    push %rdx
    push 120(%rdi)
    mov 112(%rdi), %rax
    push %rax
    push %rsi
    push 104(%rdi)
    mov %rdx, %rcx
    mov %cx, %ds
    mov %cx, %es
    mov 0(%rdi), %r15
    mov 8(%rdi), %r14
    mov 16(%rdi), %r13
    mov 24(%rdi), %r12
    mov 32(%rdi), %rbp
    mov 40(%rdi), %rbx
    mov 48(%rdi), %r10
    mov 56(%rdi), %r9
    mov 64(%rdi), %r8
    mov 72(%rdi), %rdx
    mov 80(%rdi), %rsi
    mov 96(%rdi), %rax
    mov 88(%rdi), %rdi
    iretq
#ifdef __ELF__
.size resume_user_asm,.-resume_user_asm
#endif

# Architecture

Slop/MNU uses a modular monolithic kernel for the first compatibility target. Rust owns memory-safe policy and object lifetimes; small assembly files own privilege transitions; C is reserved for clean-room source adapters for drivers whose original upstream license permits reuse.

## Compatibility planes

1. **Linux userspace ABI:** x86-64 ELF, syscall numbers, errno, signals, `/proc`, `/sys`, sockets, epoll, futex, ioctls and device nodes.
2. **Driver source portability:** a generated compatibility facade maps a documented subset of Linux driver-facing concepts onto Slop/MNU's native driver API.
3. **Hardware behavior:** PCIe, ACPI, interrupt controllers, DMA/IOMMU, USB, NVMe, networking and graphics are implemented from hardware specifications or permissively licensed references.

Binary `.ko` compatibility is intentionally not the primary design. Linux has no stable in-kernel binary ABI. A version-pinned module capsule may be researched later, but it is a VM-like compatibility product, not the native driver model.

## Kernel subsystems

- `arch`: trap/syscall/context-switch and MMU details
- `vm`: physical allocator, address spaces, copyin/copyout, mmap
- `task`: threads, processes, credentials, namespaces and signals
- `vfs`: inode/dentry/file model and filesystem registration
- `linux_abi`: translation layer, never the kernel's internal API
- `driver`: bus-neutral device graph, resources, DMA and interrupts
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/architecture.md

# Compatibility provenance ledger

Every externally observed constant, structure layout and behavior receives an entry with: interface, architecture/version, public specification or black-box test source, implementer, reviewer and tests. No implementation contributor may consult Linux implementation source for the same component during a clean-room reimplementation.

Initial ABI facts used in this scaffold:
- ELF64 little-endian x86-64 machine ID 62.
- Linux x86-64 syscall numbers: write=1, getpid=39, exit=60, uname=63, exit_group=231.
- Linux kernel errors are represented as negative errno at the raw syscall boundary.

C runtime facts used by the in-tree `libc/`:
- ISO C function names and signatures (`memcpy`, `memmove`, `memset`, `memcmp`, `strlen`, `strcmp`, `strncmp`, `strchr`, `strcpy`, `malloc`, `calloc`, `realloc`, `free`, `getenv`, and the `printf` subset): public specification ISO/IEC 9899; implemented clean-room by sloppy-coder0821 from function semantics only. Reviewer: pending. Tests: `/bin/hello-c` checks in `tools/acceptance-test.sh`.
- Linux x86-64 syscall numbers used by the wrappers (`read`=0, `write`=1, `close`=3, `mmap`=9, `openat`=257, `exit_group`=231): public Linux man-pages. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: C runtime acceptance chain.
- Wrapper constants `AT_FDCWD`=-100, `O_RDONLY`=0, `PROT_READ`=1, `PROT_WRITE`=2, `MAP_PRIVATE`=0x02, `MAP_ANONYMOUS`=0x20: public Linux man-pages. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: `openat`/`mmap` checks in `/bin/hello-c`.

Process, pipe and descriptor facts (v0.27):
- Linux x86-64 syscall numbers `pipe`=22, `dup2`=33, `clone`=56, `wait4`=61, `fcntl`=72, `dup3`=292, `pipe2`=293: public Linux man-pages. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: `/bin/hello-posix` checks in `tools/acceptance-test.sh`.
- `fork` is modeled as `clone` with `exit_signal` `SIGCHLD`=17 and the remaining arguments zero: public Linux man-pages. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: `fork` plus `wait4` round trip in `/bin/hello-posix`.
- Wait status encoding: exited child `WIFEXITED`/`WEXITSTATUS` (`status>>8&0xff`), signal death `WIFSIGNALED`/`WTERMSIG` (`status&0x7f`), `WNOHANG`=1, `WUNTRACED`=2, `ECHILD`=10: public Linux man-pages and `sys/wait.h`. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: `/bin/hello-posix` verifies exit status 7 and a user-fault status of 128+11 in earlier milestones.
- Pipe semantics: EOF on `read` when the last write description closes, `EPIPE` when the last read description closes (no `SIGPIPE` yet), shared offsets and pipe ends across `fork`, per-process descriptor tables: public Linux man-pages and pipe(7). Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: pipe read-to-EOF and content match in `/bin/hello-posix`.
- `pipe2` accepts `O_CLOEXEC`=0x80000 and ignores it (no exec-time close is implemented yet); `fstat` reports pipes as `S_IFIFO`=0x1000: public Linux man-pages. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: `/bin/hello-posix` writes through a `dup2`-mapped pipe descriptor.

Signal ABI facts (v0.29):
- Linux x86-64 syscall number `rt_sigpending`=127: public Linux man-pages. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: `/bin/hello-signal` checks in `tools/acceptance-test.sh`.
- `struct k_sigaction` field order is handler, flags, mask, restorer, each a machine word: public Linux man-pages for `rt_sigaction` and the published kernel interface description. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the `sigaction` wrapper in `libc/signal.c` and the handler check in `/bin/hello-signal`.
- `SA_NOCLDSTOP`=1, `SA_NOCLDWAIT`=2, `SA_SIGINFO`=4, `SA_RESTORER`=0x04000000, `SA_ONSTACK`=0x08000000, `SA_RESTART`=0x10000000, `SA_NODEFER`=0x40000000, `SA_RESETHAND`=0x80000000 and `SIG_DFL`=0, `SIG_IGN`=1: public Linux man-pages. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the ignored signal and the blocked signal checks in `/bin/hello-signal`.
- A handler installed without `SA_RESTORER` is rejected with `EINVAL`, and the restorer performs the `rt_sigreturn` syscall: public Linux man-pages for `sigaction` and the x86-64 ABI note that a restorer is mandatory. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the handler round trip in `/bin/hello-signal` through `libc/signal.S`.
- The signal frame places the restorer address at the bottom, the `ucontext_t` above it and the `siginfo_t` above that; the handler is entered with the signal number in RDI, the siginfo pointer in RSI and the ucontext pointer in RDX: public Linux man-pages for `sigaction` and the published x86-64 signal frame description. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the siginfo and ucontext checks in `/bin/hello-signal`.
- The `ucontext_t` layout is flags, link, stack, machine context, signal mask and the floating-point area; the machine context is the `sigcontext` with the register order named by the published `REG_*` index list (`REG_R8`=0 through `REG_CR2`=22): public libc interface description of `ucontext_t` and the published `REG_*` index list. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the instruction pointer check in `/bin/hello-signal` reads `uc_mcontext.gregs[REG_RIP]`.
- `siginfo_t` starts with `si_signo`, `si_errno` and `si_code`; a signal sent by a process reports `si_code`=`SI_USER`=0 with the sender pid and uid in the `_kill` member: public Linux man-pages for `sigaction` and `kill`. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the siginfo code and sender checks in `/bin/hello-signal`.
- Default actions: `SIGCHLD` and `SIGCONT` are ignored, `SIGSTOP` and `SIGTSTP` stop the task, every other signal terminates it: public Linux man-pages for `signal`. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP`/`SIGCONT` checks in `/bin/hello-signal`.
- A blocking `wait4` or pipe read that is interrupted by a queued signal reports `EINTR`=4: public Linux man-pages for `wait` and `read`. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the parent wait in `/bin/hello-signal` observes the interrupted wait.
- `fork` clears the pending signal set of the child and `execve` clears the pending set and the signal mask: public Linux man-pages for `fork` and `execve`. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the handler count checks in `/bin/hello-signal` run across a fork and an exec chain.
- The x86-64 `SYSRET` instruction loads RFLAGS from RAX and `IRETQ` restores RIP, CS, RFLAGS, RSP and SS while leaving RAX unchanged: AMD64 architecture manual, public processor specification. Implementer: sloppy-coder0821. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the whole acceptance chain returns from every syscall with the result in RAX and the user flags intact.

Signal ABI facts (v0.65.0):
- The x86-64 `rt_sigframe` places the restorer address in the lowest slot, the `ucontext_t` above it and the `siginfo_t` above that: public Linux man-pages for `rt_sigaction` and the published x86-64 signal frame description. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the signal acceptance program checks the handler, the siginfo and the ucontext.
- The `ucontext_t` layout is flags, link, stack, the machine context, the signal mask and the floating-point area, and the machine context is the `sigcontext` view with the published `REG_*` index list (`gregs[REG_RIP]` is the interrupted instruction pointer): public libc interface description of `ucontext_t` and the published `REG_*` index list. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the signal acceptance program reads `uc_mcontext.gregs[REG_RIP]`.
- The `siginfo_t` starts with `si_signo`, `si_errno` and `si_code`, and a signal sent by a process reports `si_code`=`SI_USER`=0 with the sender pid and uid in the `_kill` member: public Linux man-pages for `sigaction` and `kill`. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the signal acceptance program checks the siginfo code and the sender.
- `rt_sigaction` uses the Linux `k_sigaction` field order (handler, flags, restorer, mask) and a handler without a restorer cannot return: public Linux man-pages for `rt_sigaction` and the x86-64 ABI note that a restorer is mandatory. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the signal acceptance program installs a handler and returns from it.
- A delivered signal leaves the pending set, the pre-handler mask is restored when the handler returns, and `SA_RESETHAND`, `SA_NODEFER` and `SA_SIGINFO` have their Linux effect: public Linux man-pages for `sigaction` and `signal`. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the signal acceptance program covers the mask and the reset behaviour.
- A blocking `wait4` or `waitid` reports `EINTR` when a signal with a handler arrives; a signal with the default "ignore" disposition does not interrupt the wait: public Linux man-pages for `wait` and `signal`. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the signal acceptance program waits for a child that signals the parent.
- A fatal signal that a process sends to itself ends the process before the syscall returns, but only when the default disposition is in effect: public Linux man-pages for `kill`. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the signal acceptance program raises `SIGKILL` in a child.

Memory ABI facts (v0.65.0):
- A process may hold 1024 descriptors and the system may hold 512 open file descriptions: public Linux man-pages for `getrlimit` and the published `RLIMIT_NOFILE` default. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the acceptance chain opens and closes many descriptors.
- `madvise` accepts the Linux advice set: `MADV_RANDOM`=1, `MADV_SEQUENTIAL`=2, `MADV_WILLNEED`=3, `MADV_DONTNEED`=4, `MADV_HUGEPAGE`=6, `MADV_NOHUGEPAGE`=7, `MADV_FREE`=14, `MADV_DONTDUMP`=16, `MADV_WIPEONFORK`=17, `MADV_DONTFORK`=18 and `MADV_COLD`=20 are accepted, `MADV_DONTNEED` drops the pages and an unknown advice reports `EINVAL`: public Linux man-pages for `madvise`. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the differential battery exercises `madvise`.
- `rseq` reports `ENOSYS` when the kernel does not implement it, so a C library falls back to a registration-free thread-local storage path: public Linux man-pages for `rseq`. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the musl differential program starts without a registration.


Driver facts (v0.66.0):
- The virtio 1.0 device interface describes itself through vendor specific PCI capabilities (identifier 0x09) that name the common configuration, the notification area and the device configuration: the public virtio 1.0 specification. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the kernel brings up a default `virtio-blk-pci` and `virtio-net-pci` device model.
- The common configuration registers are the device feature select and word, the driver feature select and word, the queue count, the device status, the queue select, the queue size, the queue enable, the queue notification offset and the three queue addresses: the public virtio 1.0 specification. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the block and network drivers read the capacity, the hardware address and the queue state through it.
- A modern virtio device is used only after the driver asked for the `VIRTIO_F_VERSION_1` feature (bit 32) and the device confirmed the result with the `FEATURES_OK` status bit: the public virtio 1.0 specification. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the drivers refuse to start a device that does not confirm.
- The notification register of a queue sits at the notification offset multiplied by the notification multiplier, two bytes per queue: the public virtio 1.0 specification. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the UDP and TCP echo acceptance program moves packets through the network driver.
- A split virtqueue starts with the descriptor table, followed by the available ring with its flags word, and the used ring at the next page boundary: the public virtio 1.0 specification. Implementer: opencode. Reviewer: pending. Tests: the block driver reads and writes a sector over both interfaces.


Before release, legal counsel must approve the clean-room protocol, licenses, trademarks and distribution process.

# Driver compatibility strategy

"Linux drivers" are not a single stable ABI target. Slop/MNU splits the problem:

- userspace-visible device ABI compatibility: device nodes, ioctl payloads, sysfs/procfs/netlink behavior;
- source portability: recompile a legally compatible driver against a versioned Slop/MNU facade;
- hardware enablement: native Rust drivers written from vendor specifications;
- optional legacy capsule: execute a narrowly pinned driver in an isolated compatibility domain.

The facade must never expose Slop/MNU internals. It uses opaque handles, generated layout assertions and explicit lifecycle rules. Each supported Linux API profile is versioned and tested independently.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/driver-compat.md

FILE: docs/linux-compatibility.md

================================================================================
# Linux compatibility target

The first executable target is a statically linked x86-64 Linux program using a small libc startup path. Compatibility is measured at the raw syscall boundary, ELF process image, initial stack and userspace-visible device interfaces.

## Implemented bootstrap surface

`read`, `write`, `close`, `fstat`, `openat`, `newfstatat`, `lseek`, `mmap`, `mprotect`, `munmap`, `brk`, `sched_yield`, `clock_gettime`, `nanosleep`, `getrandom`, identity calls, PID/TID calls, `uname`, `arch_prctl` FS-base state, `set_tid_address`, `set_robust_list`, `prlimit64`, `execve`, `exit`, `exit_group`, `clone` (fork subset), `wait4`, `pipe`, `pipe2`, `dup2`, `dup3`, `fcntl` (minimal), `kill`, `rt_sigaction`, `rt_sigprocmask`, `rt_sigpending`, `rt_sigreturn`.

## Signal behaviour

Signals are delivered on the syscall return path. The handler frame follows the published Linux x86-64 `rt_sigframe` layout: the restorer address, then the `ucontext_t` with the machine context in the `REG_*` order, then the `siginfo_t`. `SA_SIGINFO` passes the siginfo and the ucontext, `SA_NODEFER` keeps the signal unblocked during the handler, `SA_RESETHAND` resets the action and `SA_RESTORER` is required for every installed handler. A blocked signal stays pending, `rt_sigpending` reports the pending set and `SIGCONT` resumes a stopped task. A blocking `wait4` or pipe read reports `EINTR` when a signal is queued.

## In-tree C runtime

`libc/` is a clean-room static C runtime for this ABI surface: freestanding startup, a syscall shim, string functions, a `printf` subset, a first-fit allocator over anonymous mappings, `getenv`, `errno` via `__errno_location`, and file, process, pipe, time and signal wrappers. Errors are reported through `-1` plus `errno` like POSIX libc, on top of raw syscall returns. `/bin/hello-c`, `/bin/hello-posix` and `/bin/hello-signal` are the acceptance programs built with it.

## Deliberately not claimed yet

real-time signals (`SIGRTMIN`+), `sigaltstack`, `SA_ONSTACK`, `sigqueue`, `signalfd`, `sigpending` reporting for a stopped child (`WUNTRACED` wait status), signals beyond `SIGCHLD` bookkeeping, `SIGPIPE` delivery, threads (`CLONE_VM`/`CLONE_THREAD`), copy-on-write, futex waiting/waking, `select`/`poll`/`epoll`, sockets, path lookup completeness, and dynamic linking. Returning `ENOSYS` is preferable to silently violating Linux semantics.

## Acceptance gate

A release only advances to "runs Linux binaries" when the same statically linked test ELF produces matching stdout, exit status, syscall results and errno values on Linux and Slop/MNU under an automated differential harness.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/linux-compatibility.md

FILE: docs/milestone10.md

# Milestone 10: first process-owned CR3

The acceptance process no longer inserts user PTEs directly into the firmware root. A new PML4 frame is allocated, the live root is copied to preserve the executing PE image, kernel stacks, serial I/O path, BootInfo and identity-accessible RAM, and dedicated user slots are cleared before mapping.

The test ELF is linked at 4 TiB (`0x0000040000000000`), giving it PML4 slot 8. The stack remains near the top of the lower canonical half in slot 255. Both slots are private in the new root. Layouts that use slot 0 or cross the image slot are rejected.

Immediately before IRETQ, CR3 is loaded with the process root. The syscall kernel path remains mapped because the rest of the firmware root was copied. This is an isolation step, not a final kernel address-space design: low firmware mappings and most other firmware PML4 entries are still inherited. The next stage is a deliberate kernel-only mapping set and a physical direct map rather than wholesale firmware inheritance.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone10.md

FILE: docs/milestone11.md
SIZE: 1173 bytes
SHA-256: 17588f19f4370b709a150a2c6532cef9382d61ff2576cf2762ad84bff3be4487
================================================================================
# Milestone 11: process exit and address-space reclamation

The first Linux process now has an explicit lifetime. Before ring-3 entry, the kernel records the bootstrap CR3, process CR3, ELF PML4 slot, and stack PML4 slot. `exit_group` reaches `task::exit`, which switches back to the bootstrap CR3 before touching page-table frames owned by the process.

Each private slot is then walked through PDPT, PD, PT, and leaf PTE levels. User frames are returned to the physical allocator, empty PT/PD/PDPT frames are returned, the PML4 entry is cleared, and finally the process PML4 frame itself is recycled. Shared firmware/kernel entries are never traversed or released because only slots cleared before process construction are registered as private.

The teardown is intentionally limited to 4 KiB mappings. A large-page entry in a private slot is rejected. When huge pages are introduced, teardown must handle their ownership explicitly.

`tools/run-qemu.sh` provides the first reproducible acceptance invocation for QEMU with OVMF, one CPU, serial output, and the generated FAT directory. Paths to OVMF firmware can be overridden with `OVMF_CODE` and `OVMF_VARS_TEMPLATE`.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone11.md

FILE: docs/milestone12.md
SIZE: 1170 bytes
SHA-256: 43882c07a24b12630f5985f2166b686acbe2c976372654083a6b470e8b1e2500
================================================================================
# Milestone 12: user-fault containment and ABI self-test

CPU exceptions now pass vector, error code, RIP, and CS to Rust. Page fault, general protection, and double fault have dedicated assembly stubs because the CPU already pushes an error code for those exceptions. Exceptions originating at CPL3 are recognized through `CS & 3` and terminate only the active Linux process. The kernel first records a signal-like status, switches back to the bootstrap CR3, reclaims the private address space, and halts the acceptance CPU. Ring-0 exceptions remain fatal kernel exceptions.

The static Linux ELF now begins with a negative test: `write(1, 0x1234, 8)` must return raw `-EFAULT` (`-14`). It then performs the valid console write and checks the returned byte count. Any mismatch emits a failure message on fd 2 and exits with status 1. Success emits `hello from a Linux ELF on Slop/MNU; EFAULT passed` and calls `exit_group(0)`.

This improves the meaning of a successful boot. It demonstrates not only ring-3 execution and SYSCALL/SYSRET, but also Linux raw errno behavior, page-table-backed user-pointer validation, a successful user-buffer copy, and process teardown.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone12.md

FILE: docs/milestone13.md
SIZE: 1233 bytes
SHA-256: 1bfa7d60c6a7d0b9f868130b7a21279d454a274f7fbf2d3cdf161dd7e4b9ab62

# Milestone 13: ABI hardening and build preflight

The UEFI loader is compiled with the Microsoft x64 ABI while the kernel entry is explicitly SysV64. The transition stub now subtracts eight bytes before `call`, so the SysV callee observes the required stack alignment. BootInfo remains passed in RDI.

Exception stubs now distinguish additional CPU-error-code vectors: invalid TSS (10), segment not present (11), stack-segment fault (12), general protection (13), page fault (14), alignment check (17), and double fault (8). Treating these as no-error-code exceptions would shift RIP and CS decoding and could misclassify a kernel fault as a user fault. DF is cleared before calling Rust.

`tools/check-source.sh` checks critical ABI and ownership connections without compiling. `tools/check-toolchain.sh` verifies all required host commands and runs the source checks. The UEFI build invokes this preflight before producing artifacts.

The Rust cross-target build now includes `compiler_builtins` with memory intrinsics through `-Zbuild-std`. This reduces dependence on host CRT implementations when linking the freestanding PE/COFF image, while the loader still supplies the basic C memory symbols needed by Clang-generated code.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone13.md

FILE: docs/milestone14.md

# Milestone 14: deterministic acceptance result

A halted VM is ambiguous: it may mean a successful `exit_group`, a kernel deadlock, or a fatal exception. The bootstrap test now writes a result byte to QEMU's `isa-debug-exit` device after process teardown. Guest value `0x10` denotes success, `0x11` denotes a nonzero Linux exit status, and `0x12` denotes a contained user CPU fault.

`tools/acceptance-test.sh` runs QEMU under a timeout, saves serial output to `build/acceptance-serial.log`, and requires all of the following:

- QEMU host status 33, corresponding to guest value `0x10`.
- The Linux ELF success marker containing `EFAULT passed`.
- A zero task exit status.
- Confirmation that the process address space was reclaimed with vector zero.
- Absence of the ABI failure marker and kernel-exception marker.

This turns the first Linux-program goal into a machine-checkable gate rather than a visual inspection of serial output. The actual guest execution remains to be performed in the user's build environment.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone14.md

FILE: docs/milestone15.md
SIZE: 799 bytes
SHA-256: 64f6b94c7bf2a8a4343e647d6e351ef38e5444ed06e91c1e48a3cf4a12968f05

# Milestone 15: reliable serial acceptance output

The automated gate depends on the final process-reclamation marker being visible before QEMU exits. The earlier serial writer sent bytes directly to COM1 and could overrun the UART FIFO or terminate the VM before the last bytes left the transmitter.

The console now polls the line-status register before every byte, normalizes newline to CRLF, and exposes `console::flush`. Polling loops are bounded so a missing UART cannot hang the kernel forever. The QEMU debug-exit path drains the transmitter before writing to port `0xf4`.

This change does not implement interrupt-driven serial I/O. Polling is appropriate for the single-core bootstrap and deterministic acceptance log; a queued IRQ-driven console belongs after APIC/IOAPIC initialization.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone15.md

FILE: docs/milestone16.md
SIZE: 969 bytes
SHA-256: bad2dee4ce5d0d65d51e2fa93d42bbcf1691b3f0adf405777445965f441e0a93
================================================================================
# Milestone 16: CPU capability gate and write protection

The bootstrap now validates architectural prerequisites instead of assuming every x86-64 environment supports the selected entry path. Extended CPUID checks require SYSCALL/SYSRET and NX before their MSRs or PTE bits are used. Optional 1 GiB page, SMEP, and SMAP capabilities are reported for later stages.

CR0.WP is enabled during CPU initialization. With WP set, ring-0 writes honor read-only page-table entries. This is required before W^X and immutable kernel mappings can be treated as real protection rather than user-only protection.

SMEP and SMAP are detected but deliberately not enabled yet. SMEP would require every kernel instruction path to be mapped supervisor-only, while the current process CR3 inherits firmware mappings. SMAP requires explicit STAC/CLAC handling around user memory access. Both will be enabled after the kernel-owned mapping set and direct-map replace firmware inheritance.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone16.md

FILE: docs/milestone17.md
SIZE: 2633 bytes
SHA-256: ed6b916b302095713d70fddf489e215286375d6ab0b05d7348388988066c6d41
================================================================================
# Milestone 17: interrupt-controller and timer foundation

The UEFI loader now locates ACPI 1.0/2.0 RSDP entries in the system configuration table, preferring ACPI 2.0, and passes the pointer through BootInfo. The kernel validates RSDP, root table, and MADT checksums before consuming controller addresses. MADT parsing records the Local APIC address, first IOAPIC, ISA interrupt-source overrides, and Local APIC address override.

The bootstrap masks both 8259 PICs, enables xAPIC through IA32_APIC_BASE, enables the Local APIC software bit, masks LINT0/LINT1, and masks every IOAPIC redirection entry. External device IRQs therefore cannot enter until an explicit route is installed. The Local APIC timer runs periodically on vector 0x40. Its assembly entry preserves general-purpose registers, calls a Rust tick handler, writes EOI, restores registers, and returns with IRETQ.

Initial user RFLAGS now includes IF (`0x202`). The acceptance ELF can therefore be interrupted while running in ring 3, with hardware stack switching through TSS.RSP0. Kernel entry through SYSCALL still masks IF using IA32_FMASK, so the current global syscall stack cannot be recursively used by timer preemption.

The timer count is intentionally uncalibrated. It proves interrupt delivery but is not yet a time source. HPET or PIT calibration is required before ticks can represent nanoseconds or drive a fair scheduler quantum.

`IrqSpinLock` saves IF, disables local interrupts while held, and restores the previous IF state on release. This is the required locking primitive for run queues and timer queues shared with interrupt context.

Before entering user mode, the kernel performs a bounded Local APIC timer self-test. It enables IF, executes HLT, receives periodic timer interrupts, disables IF again, and requires the tick counter to advance. The acceptance harness now requires the marker `local APIC timer and EOI passed`, making timer delivery part of the automated gate.

`route_isa` converts an ISA IRQ through MADT interrupt-source overrides, applies active-low and level-trigger flags, selects the bootstrap Local APIC as destination, and programs the matching IOAPIC redirection entry. All routes remain masked unless a driver explicitly requests otherwise.

The timer gate uses TSS IST2 and a dedicated 64 KiB IRQ stack. This avoids corrupting the global SYSCALL kernel stack when a timer interrupt arrives while Rust syscall code is running. The assembly entry also adds an eight-byte alignment slot before calling Rust, because the CPU interrupt frame plus fifteen saved registers otherwise leaves the SysV call site misaligned.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone17.md

FILE: docs/milestone18.md
SIZE: 1705 bytes
SHA-256: 1c5af908b36e2288a858d6d1be2663f8b86ff8c2ddb13fe96ef13bd2e67cf29d
================================================================================
# Milestone 18: scheduler and kernel-thread foundation

Slop/MNU now has a single-CPU fixed-capacity scheduler with eight task slots. Each task records PID, scheduling state, saved kernel RSP, kernel-stack top, wake tick, and exit status. Task states include unused, running, runnable, sleeping, and zombie. PIDs are allocated dynamically from 2 while the bootstrap task remains PID 1.

Each task slot owns a 32 KiB aligned kernel stack. New kernel threads receive a synthetic switch frame containing the SysV64 callee-preserved register set and a return target for `kernel_thread_trampoline`. The initial stack includes an alignment bias so the trampoline observes the required SysV function-entry alignment.

`switch_context` saves RBP, RBX, and R12-R15, stores the old RSP, loads the next RSP, restores the next task's preserved state, and returns into that task. The scheduler selects runnable tasks round-robin while holding `IrqSpinLock`, but releases the queue lock before performing the assembly switch.

The Local APIC timer increments ticks, wakes sleeping tasks and sets an atomic `need_resched` flag. It deliberately does not switch tasks inside the IRQ handler yet. Full timer preemption requires an interrupt-frame-aware return path capable of replacing the interrupted context safely. Cooperative `yield_now` and `sleep_until` are active now.

A boot-time self-test creates two kernel threads. Each advances a counter, yields, resumes, advances again, and exits into zombie state. The test passes only after both counters reach two, demonstrating separate kernel stacks and bidirectional context switching. The automated QEMU gate now requires `scheduler: context switch self-test passed`.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone18.md

================================================================================
FILE: docs/milestone19.md
SIZE: 1413 bytes
SHA-256: 9d5786806261832a35defb84ae5c6f898134e6116ad0c74cb7145f3d80c5e263
================================================================================
# Milestone 19: per-task architecture state and TLS foundation

Task descriptors now carry the active CR3, FS base, kernel-stack top, saved kernel RSP, PID, state, wake tick, and exit status. Before a cooperative context switch, the scheduler applies the next task's architecture state: CR3 when different, IA32_FS_BASE, TSS.RSP0, and the SYSCALL entry stack pointer.

The bootstrap task is initialized with the actual 64 KiB ring-0 stack configured during descriptor setup. Kernel threads continue to use their own 32 KiB stacks. This distinction matters because a later user-to-kernel privilege transition must land on the kernel stack belonging to the selected task.

`arch_prctl(ARCH_SET_FS)` now updates both the current task record and IA32_FS_BASE. `ARCH_GET_FS` continues to copy the stored value to user memory. On every scheduler switch the saved value is restored, establishing the minimum TLS behavior needed by libc once user tasks become schedulable.

The self-test now assigns FS base `0x1000` to one kernel thread and `0x2000` to another. Each thread yields, resumes, reads IA32_FS_BASE, and requires its own value. Passing the test demonstrates that context switches update task-local TLS state in addition to CPU stack state.

This is still a single-CPU design. GS-based per-CPU state and SWAPGS are not enabled, and timer IRQs set `need_resched` without replacing the interrupted return frame.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone19.md
