FILE: docs/milestone20.md
SIZE: 1465 bytes
SHA-256: 5f269367e15553ab6928228fb19a8a5b89ddfd4ea5c6a6a1a1050319c21fb764
================================================================================
# Milestone 20: wait queues and zombie reaping

Tasks now record a parent PID when created. The Linux `getppid` syscall reads this scheduler relationship instead of returning a constant zero. The bootstrap task remains parentless, while kernel threads created by it receive PPID 1.

A fixed-capacity `WaitQueue` stores up to eight waiting PIDs under `IrqSpinLock`. `sleep` registers the current task and blocks it; `wake_one` removes one waiter and changes its task state from sleeping to runnable; `wake_all` repeats that operation until the queue is empty. This is the basic synchronization primitive needed by future futexes, pipes, device reads, and child-exit waits.

The scheduler now exposes `block_current`, `wake_pid`, `reap_zombie`, and `wait_child`. Reaping verifies the caller is the recorded parent, returns the child's exit status, clears the task descriptor, clears its stored kernel-thread entry, and makes the fixed task slot reusable. The current bounded `wait_child` yields cooperatively and returns `EAGAIN` if no matching child becomes a zombie during the scan. A fully blocking Linux `wait4` will use a child-exit wait queue rather than this bounded polling loop.

The boot self-test still runs two kernel threads with distinct FS bases, but now also waits for both to become zombies, validates their zero status, and reclaims both task slots. The automated acceptance marker is `scheduler: context, FS base and zombie reap self-test passed`.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone20.md

FILE: docs/milestone21.md

# Milestone 21: read-only initramfs, VFS and file descriptors

The UEFI image now embeds an initramfs rather than embedding the acceptance ELF as the initrd payload directly. `tools/build-initramfs.py` creates the allocation-free NSAR format with `/init` as an executable regular file and `/etc/motd` as a read-only data file. The UEFI loader exports the complete archive through the existing BootInfo initrd fields.

The kernel validates the NSAR magic, entry count, name length, absolute path form, metadata bounds, data bounds and alignment before publishing any inode. The mounted filesystem supports exact absolute-path lookup and offset-based reads. Boot no longer passes the initrd bytes directly to the ELF loader: it mounts the archive, resolves `/init` through VFS, then passes that inode's bytes to the existing transactional static ELF loader.

A fixed 32-entry FD table initializes descriptors 0, 1 and 2 as console endpoints. `openat(AT_FDCWD, absolute_path, O_RDONLY)` allocates descriptors 3 and above. Each regular-file descriptor carries its own offset. `read`, `lseek`, `close`, `fstat`, and `newfstatat` are connected to initramfs inodes, while writes remain limited to stdout and stderr.

The acceptance ELF now opens `/etc/motd`, reads it through fd 3+, writes the returned bytes to stdout, closes the descriptor, performs the existing EFAULT test, and exits successfully. A kernel-side mount test also resolves `/etc/motd` and verifies its exact contents before entering ring 3.

This is a deliberately read-only first VFS. It does not yet provide directories as first-class inodes, relative lookup, cwd, symlinks, `getdents64`, descriptor duplication, per-task FD-table cloning, or writable tmpfs pages.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone21.md

FILE: docs/milestone22.md
SIZE: 1665 bytes
SHA-256: de1544f4bd9a8a7c7769e3cdf116a8154c6f50e8481434e6a8d6bc3f8189c56e
================================================================================
# Milestone 22: hardware-backed anonymous VM and brk

Anonymous private mappings now allocate one physical frame per page, clear the frame through the bootstrap physical-access mapping, and install a user PTE in the currently active process page table. PTE permissions are derived from Linux protection bits: writable follows `PROT_WRITE`, executable pages clear NX, and non-executable pages set NX. Every mapping remains user-accessible.

`mmap` first reserves a VMA and then maps pages. If frame allocation or page-table insertion fails partway through, all successfully mapped pages are removed and recycled, and the VMA reservation is rolled back. `MAP_FIXED` currently rejects overlap rather than replacing an existing mapping, which avoids destructive partial replacement until VMA splitting is implemented.

`mprotect` updates each leaf PTE through `protect_page`, including TLB invalidation, then updates the VMA ledger. `munmap` removes every leaf PTE, invalidates it, recycles the frame, and removes the exact VMA. Partial VMA ranges are not accepted yet.

Upward `brk` growth reserves an anonymous read/write VMA and maps zero-filled pages between the old and new page boundary. Shrinking is conservatively rejected until adjacent brk extents can be merged and split transactionally. Linux semantics are preserved by returning the old break on failure.

The ring-3 acceptance program now requests a writable anonymous page, verifies zero fill, writes and reads a value, changes the page to read-only with `mprotect`, unmaps it, grows brk by one page, verifies zero fill, and writes and reads heap data. Its success marker is `VFS, VM and EFAULT passed`.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone22.md

================================================================================
FILE: docs/milestone23.md
SIZE: 1876 bytes
SHA-256: 5781c5f9259aba9f44fd55ff767ef08f2af71ab042d2568b874662c652e9a064
================================================================================
# Milestone 23: transactional static execve

Slop/MNU now dispatches Linux x86-64 syscall 59 and resolves the executable path through the read-only initramfs VFS. The syscall copies the path, argv pointer vector, argument strings, envp pointer vector, and environment strings through page-table-backed usercopy before changing the active address space. The bootstrap limits are eight arguments, eight environment entries, and 127 bytes per string.

The ELF commit path accepts caller-provided argv, envp, and execfn values. It clones the active kernel-visible mappings into a new PML4, clears the executable and user-stack slots, validates and installs the static ELF, creates the Linux initial stack, and returns a complete candidate image. Any error before this point leaves the old process active and returns an errno.

After the candidate is complete, the kernel activates its CR3, updates the task and process-runtime ownership records, resets brk, TLS, clear-child-tid and the VMA ledger, then reclaims the old executable slot, stack slot and PML4. The syscall entry notices an exec-pending flag, replaces the saved SYSRET RIP with the new ELF entry, uses the new user RSP, and clears the flag. A successful exec therefore never resumes at the old syscall instruction.

The initramfs now contains `/init`, `/bin/hello`, and `/etc/motd`. `/init` invokes `execve("/bin/hello", ["hello"], ["SLOP_MNU_EXEC=1"])`. The second ELF validates argc, argv and envp directly on its initial stack, then runs the existing VFS and hardware-backed VM tests before exiting.

This implementation is intentionally static-ELF-only. It does not yet implement close-on-exec descriptors, signal disposition reset, credentials, shebang handling, dynamic interpreters, multithreaded exec, arbitrarily large argument blocks, or complete cleanup of non-image private mappings created before exec.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone23.md

# Milestone 25: integrated user task, time, sleep, and random

The boot user ELF is a runnable child task selected by the cooperative scheduler. It owns a kernel stack and CR3, enters ring 3 through a user-task trampoline, and returns to PID 1 as a zombie after exit or a user fault.

Linux syscall paths now include bootstrap implementations of `clock_gettime`, `nanosleep`, and `getrandom`. Time uses Local APIC ticks with a temporary 1 ms conversion, and random output comes from a non-cryptographic xorshift64 generator. These support libc bring-up but are not yet production timekeeping or secure entropy.

The next acceptance gate is a genuinely static C/libc executable reaching `main`, not another hand-written assembly program.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Milestone 26: clean-room static C runtime

The acceptance chain now ends in a C executable built from the in-tree `libc/` runtime instead of hand-written assembly. `/init` execs `/bin/hello` (assembly ABI checks), which then uses Linux `execve` to hand the process to `/bin/hello-c`. The C program reaches `main` with `argc`, `argv` and `envp`, exercises the runtime, prints `c runtime: passed` and returns 0.

The runtime covers freestanding startup (`libc/start.S`), a syscall shim translating the System V C calling convention to the Linux x86-64 syscall ABI (`libc/syscall.S`), first-fit `malloc`/`calloc`/`realloc`/`free` over anonymous mappings, string functions, a `printf` subset, `getenv` over the initial environment, and `openat`/`read`/`write`/`close` wrappers. The build uses Clang and LLD only; no host libc or GCC is involved.

Bootstrap limits are deliberate: only the listed libc subset exists, error reporting uses raw negative return values rather than `errno`, stdout is unbuffered, and `printf` supports `%s %d %u %x %c %p %%` without width or length modifiers. The linker script gained the large-code-model `.ltext`/`.ldata`/`.lbss` input sections so C objects can use the 64 TiB user ELF base.

The next acceptance gate is running an existing statically linked libc program (for example a musl-linked PID 1 and shell) unmodified through the Linux ABI.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone26.md

FILE: docs/milestone27.md
SIZE: 2603 bytes
SHA-256: 320a6b07d6555c4f8b09374435220fa59e1b1799c845bce7e1235c64b5abe638
================================================================================
# Milestone 27: real processes, pipes and wait4

The acceptance chain now runs a multi-process C program. `/bin/hello-posix` creates a pipe, forks, and the child maps the pipe write end onto stdout with `dup2`, writes `posix: child message 42` and exits with status 7. The parent reads the pipe to EOF, compares the bytes exactly, reaps the child with `wait4` and verifies `WIFEXITED`/`WEXITSTATUS`. The in-tree `libc/` grew a minimal `errno` (`__errno_location` plus wrappers that translate negative returns to `-1`/`errno`) and `fork`, `pipe`, `dup2` and `waitpid` wrappers.

## Process model

- `kernel/process.rs` owns the complete per-process state: CR3 and bootstrap CR3, image/stack/mmap PML4 slots, the VMA map, brk, the clear-child-TID pointer and the file-descriptor table. Teardown activates the bootstrap CR3 first, then destroys the private slots and frees the root.
- `fork` deep-copies the three private PML4 slots so parent and child have fully separated address spaces (no copy-on-write yet). The child resumes at the saved syscall return address with `rax=0` through `resume_user_asm` in `arch/x86_64/user.S`.
- Tasks gained a process group ID, a terminating-signal field and a wake-pending latch. Wait queue users register before blocking, and the latch closes the lost-wakeup window between registration and sleep.
- `wait4` supports positive PIDs, `-1`, `0` (own process group) and `<-pid`, with `WNOHANG` and `WUNTRACED`. Exit status uses the Linux wait encoding, including signal termination, and `ECHILD` is returned when no child matches.

## File descriptors and pipes

- `kernel/fd.rs` now stores shared open-file descriptions with reference counts. Descriptor tables are per-process, while offsets and pipe ends follow the shared description. `fork` increments references and teardown releases them.
- `kernel/pipe.rs` implements 16 fixed pipes with 4 KiB ring buffers and blocking `read`/`write`: EOF when the last writer closes, `EPIPE` when the last reader closes. `SIGPIPE` is not delivered yet.
- `dup2`/`dup3`, `pipe`/`pipe2`, and a minimal `fcntl` (only `F_DUPFD` and the no-op descriptor-flag forms) complete the descriptor surface. `execve` keeps descriptors open across an image replacement, now with an 8-page (32 KiB) user stack.

## Deliberate limits

No `CLONE_THREAD`/`CLONE_VM` (threads), no copy-on-write, no signals beyond `fork`'s `SIGCHLD` bookkeeping, no `SIGPIPE`, no `select`/`poll`/`epoll`, and fixed table sizes (8 tasks, 32 FDs per process, 64 open descriptions, 16 pipes). Returning `ENOSYS` or `EINVAL` remains preferable to fabricating behavior.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone27.md

FILE: docs/milestone28.md

SHA-256: b9181332762534fe736d388762796178f985e40a3b836cb5c65537fc48f9a747
================================================================================
# Milestone 28: signal ABI foundation

The kernel now accepts the Linux signal syscall surface: `kill`, `rt_sigaction`, `rt_sigprocmask` and `rt_sigreturn`. Per-process pending and blocked signal masks live in `kernel/signal.rs` alongside the handler table. `Process` carries the mask fields so `fork` and `execve` can preserve or reset them.

## Signal syscalls

- `kill(pid, sig)` delivers a signal to a task; `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP` are never blocked.
- `rt_sigaction(sig, act, oldact)` installs or queries a handler with `SIG_DFL`/`SIG_IGN` and flag support.
- `rt_sigprocmask(how, set, oldset)` blocks, unblocks or replaces the mask.
- `rt_sigreturn` is accepted for handler return trampolines.

## Delivery path

`signal_pending_check` runs before `SYSRET` in `arch/x86_64/syscall.S`. When a signal is pending the kernel builds a sigframe on the user stack and enters the handler through `signal_deliver_asm`. Default-action signals (`SIGPIPE`, `SIGKILL`, `SIGTERM`) terminate the process when no handler is installed.

## Deliberate limits

No `SA_SIGINFO` frames, no `signalfd`, no `sigaltstack`, no real-time signals (`SIGRTMIN`+), and `rt_sigreturn` does not yet restore the full saved context. Returning `ENOSYS` or `EINVAL` remains preferable to fabricating behavior.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone28.md

# Milestone 29: signal delivery and a correct syscall return

Two defects blocked the Linux userspace ABI. Both are fixed here.

## The syscall return path

`arch/x86_64/syscall.S` returned to user space with `sysretq`. `SYSRET` loads
RFLAGS from RAX, and RAX holds the syscall result, so every syscall replaced
the user flags with the return value. A return value with bit 1 clear also
raised `#GP`, and the cleared interrupt flag stopped the timer from waking the
process again.

The return now uses `iretq`, the same mechanism the kernel already uses for the
first user entry and for a fork resume. `IRETQ` restores RIP, CS, RFLAGS, RSP
and SS and leaves RAX alone, so the syscall result and the user flags survive
at the same time. The saved user RFLAGS live in the frame slot that the
`SYSCALL` instruction filled with R11.

## The syscall entry state

The user stack pointer and the register frame of the running syscall lived in
two global words. A task that blocked inside a syscall, for example in
`wait4` or in a pipe read, therefore resumed with the stack pointer of the
task that ran in between, and the return to user space used the wrong stack.
Both words now belong to the task: the scheduler stores them with the task it
switches away from and restores them with the task it switches to.

## Signal delivery

`kernel/signal.rs` builds the published Linux x86-64 `rt_sigframe` on the user
stack:

- the restorer address occupies the lowest slot, so a handler that returns with
  `ret` lands in the restorer;
- the `ucontext_t` follows, with the machine context in the published `REG_*`
  register order;
- the `siginfo_t` closes the frame.

The handler is entered with the signal number in RDI, the siginfo pointer in
RSI and the ucontext pointer in RDX. `rt_sigreturn` reads the frame back,
restores the registers, the flags, the stack pointer and the signal mask, and
returns to the interrupted code.

Signal behaviour now follows the Linux rules:

- `rt_sigaction` uses the `k_sigaction` layout (handler, flags, mask,
  restorer) and rejects a handler without `SA_RESTORER`;
- `rt_sigprocmask` reads the mask from the user pointer instead of treating
  the pointer as the mask;
- `SA_SIGINFO` delivers the siginfo and the ucontext, `SA_NODEFER` keeps the
  signal unblocked, `SA_RESETHAND` resets the action after the delivery;
- `SIG_IGN` discards the signal, `SIGCHLD` and `SIGCONT` are ignored by
  default, `SIGSTOP` and `SIGTSTP` stop the task and every other signal
  terminates it;
- `kill` reaches the requested process or process group instead of the caller;
- a blocked signal stays pending and `rt_sigpending` reports the pending set;
- a blocking `wait4` or pipe read reports `EINTR` when a signal is queued.

`fork` clears the pending set of the child and `execve` clears the pending set
and the mask, like Linux does.

## Acceptance

`/bin/hello-signal` installs a `SA_SIGINFO` handler, takes a signal from a
child process, checks the siginfo and the ucontext, verifies that an ignored
signal is discarded, that a blocked signal stays pending until the mask is
cleared, that the default action of `SIGKILL` kills a child and that a stopped
child continues after `SIGCONT`.

`tools/check-abi.py` checks the published layouts of `k_sigaction`,
`sigcontext`, `ucontext_t` and `siginfo_t` and the `REG_*` index list against
the kernel structures and the libc header. The gate needs no compiler, so a
layout drift is caught before a build.

## Deliberate limits

Signals are delivered in signal number order, not in arrival order. There is
no `sigaltstack`, no real-time signal, no `SA_ONSTACK`, no `sigqueue`, no
`signalfd` and no `WUNTRACED` reporting for a stopped child. The kernel does
not save the floating-point state, so the `ucontext` reports a zeroed area.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone29.md

FILE: docs/milestone30.md

================================================================================
# Milestone 30: complete signal delivery

Signals are no longer a bookkeeping stub: pending, blocked and handler state is
per-process, and the kernel delivers real handlers to user mode.

## Per-process signal state

`Process` carries `pending`, `sigmask`, a 64-entry handler table and the address
of the active sigframe. `Action` is `#[repr(C)]` with the Linux x86-64
`kernel_sigaction` layout (handler, flags, restorer, mask), so `rt_sigaction`
copies the user structure verbatim. `kill` resolves the target through the
scheduler and marks that task's pending mask; the previous `with_current_process`
mistake that signalled only the caller is gone.

## Delivery path

Before `SYSRET`, `syscall_entry` calls `signal_setup_frame`. The function:

1. Pops the next pending, unblocked signal. `SIG_IGN` drops it, `SIG_DFL` runs
   the default disposition (`SIGHUP`, `SIGINT`, `SIGQUIT`, `SIGILL`, `SIGABRT`,
   `SIGBUS`, `SIGFPE`, `SIGKILL`, `SIGSEGV`, `SIGPIPE`, `SIGALRM`, `SIGTERM`,
   `SIGUSR1`, `SIGUSR2` terminate; the rest are ignored), and any other handler
   number enters user mode.
2. Builds a 152-byte sigframe on the user stack: the interrupted register set,
   `rip`, `rflags` (with `IF` forced on), `rsp`, signal number, magic and the
   pre-handler mask. A restorer return slot sits *below* the sigframe so handler
   pushes grow downward without touching it.
3. Rewrites the in-flight kernel frame: `rcx` becomes the handler entry, `rdi`
   the signal number, and `SYSCALL_USER_RSP` points at the return slot.

The handler returns to `__slop_sigrestore`, which runs the `rt_sigreturn`
syscall. `signal_restore_frame` validates the magic, rewrites the kernel frame
from the sigframe and sets `SIGRET_PENDING`; the exit path then restores the
interrupted `rax` and `sysretq`s back to the interrupted point.

Blocking the delivered signal while its handler runs, `SA_RESETHAND`,
`SA_NODEFER`, and restoring the pre-handler mask on return are implemented.
`SIGCHLD` is delivered to the parent on child exit, and `SIGPIPE` is raised on
`EPIPE`.

## SysV stack alignment

`syscall_entry` runs at an 8-mod-16 stack offset after the saved frame is
reached. Calling the Rust frame rewriter without padding made its SSE spills
fault with `#GP`; the call site now pads with one `push %rax`.

## libc

`libc/signal.c` gained `__slop_sigrestore`, and `sigaction` translates the
glibc/musl user structure to the kernel layout, installing the restorer
automatically. `sys/wait.h` gained `WIFSIGNALED`/`WTERMSIG`. `/bin/hello-signal`
exercises handler installation, self-delivery, `sigprocmask` blocking and
unblocking, cross-process `kill`, and default-action termination.

## Deliberate limits

No `SA_SIGINFO` frames, no `signalfd`, no `sigaltstack`, no real-time signals,
no job-control stops (`SIGSTOP`/`SIGTSTP` are accepted but not honoured), and
`wait4` status encoding stays minimal. Nested handlers reuse the single sigframe
slot, which nested `SA_NODEFER` handlers could clobber.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone30.md

================================================================================
# Milestone 31: futex, writable VFS and directory listing

Three subsystems that standalone Linux programs lean on now work: futex, file
creation with a writable backing store, and `getdents64`.

## Futex

`kernel/futex.rs` implements `FUTEX_WAIT` and `FUTEX_WAKE` (with the
`FUTEX_PRIVATE_FLAG` bit honoured as a no-op qualifier). A fixed 64-entry waiter
table keyed by user address backs both operations:

- `FUTEX_WAIT` reads the u32 at the user address and returns `EAGAIN` when it
  differs from the caller's expected value, matching the Linux value race
  check. Otherwise the task registers a waiter and blocks; a waker clears the
  slot, and a timeout converts the absolute `timespec` deadline into a tick
  wake. `ETIMEDOUT` is returned when the deadline elapses first.
- `FUTEX_WAKE` clears up to `count` waiters on the address and wakes them,
  returning how many were woken.

`REQUEUE`, `CMP_REQUEUE` and `PI` variants return `ENOSYS`. A non-aligned or
null address returns `EFAULT`.

## Writable VFS

`Inode` gained a `capacity` field. `create` allocates and zeroes a physical
frame as the backing page, so created files hold up to 4 KiB, and `write`
appends at the open-file offset, growing `size`. `unlink` frees the backing
page and removes the node. `openat` now accepts `O_CREAT` (creating the node
when missing) and the full Linux open-flag range, and `write` on an inode
descriptor goes through the VFS. `unlinkat` was added, handling the plain
unlink form. `mount_initramfs` preserves `S_IFDIR` modes from the archive, so
directory nodes load as directories.

## getdents64

`vfs::readdir` now enumerates the entries directly below a directory through a
callback, and `sys_getdents64` renders `linux_dirent64` records (ino, offset,
reclen, type, name) using the descriptor offset as a cursor across calls. The
initramfs carries `/bin` and `/etc` directory nodes.

## libc

`read`, `write`, `close` and `openat` now funnel failures through `sys_fail` so
`errno` is set; previously they returned the raw negative syscall value. Added
`unlink`, `getdents64`, `struct dirent64`, the `O_CREAT`/`O_DIRECTORY` flags
and the common `E*` errno constants.

`/bin/hello-file` covers the new surface: create/write/read-back/unlink,
`getdents64` over `/bin`, and the `FUTEX_WAIT` `EAGAIN` path.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone31.md

# Milestone 32: Linux syscall surface completion

The syscall surface used by standalone Linux binaries is now largely covered.
This milestone adds the calls a musl-linked static program reaches for at
startup or during normal I/O, plus the ones that were previously stubbed or
missing.

## New syscalls

- `statx` — renders the 128-byte `struct statx` from a path or, with
  `AT_EMPTY_PATH`, from a descriptor.
- `mremap` — grows or shrinks an anonymous mapping: allocate the destination,
  copy the live bytes through user mappings, release the source. `MREMAP_MAYMOVE`
  is the only accepted flag.
- `waitid` — generalises `wait4` over `P_PID`/`P_PGID`/`P_ALL` selectors and
  fills a `siginfo` record with the signal number, code, pid and status.
- `readlinkat` — resolves `/proc/self/exe` to the image path recorded by the
  last `execve`.
- `faccessat`/`faccessat2`/`access` — existence and permission probing against
  the VFS.
- `execveat` — delegates to `execve`; `AT_EMPTY_PATH` returns `ENOSYS`.
- `sched_getaffinity` — reports the single online CPU.
- `poll`/`ppoll` — readiness over the descriptor kinds the kernel has, with a
  timeout that sleeps and reports zero ready rather than blocking forever.
- `pread64`/`pwrite64` — positional I/O that ignores the file offset, backed by
  new `read_off`/`write_off` VFS entry points that resolve the node by inode
  number so a grown size is visible.
- `clone3` — treats every request as a fork; the `CLONE_VM`/thread families are
  rejected with `EINVAL`.
- `rt_sigsuspend` — installs the mask and yields.
- `madvise`, `membarrier`, `rseq`, `pkey_alloc`, `sigaltstack` — accepted no-ops
  that return success, so libraries that probe for them at startup proceed.
- `getdents` — delegates to `getdents64`.

## readv/writev

Both were capped at eight 256-byte chunks. They now walk every vector in the
caller's array with 512-byte chunks, accumulating the total the way Linux does.

## libc

Added `struct iovec` with `readv`/`writev`, `pread64`/`pwrite64`, `access`,
`faccessat`, `readlinkat`, `mremap`, `madvise`, `membarrier`, `munmap`,
`mprotect` declarations, `strstr`, and the `MADV_NORMAL` constant. All wrappers
funnel failures through `sys_fail` so `errno` is set.

`/bin/hello-syscall` exercises the new surface: a `writev`/`readv` pipe round
trip, `pread64`/`pwrite64` with offset independence, `access` on present and
absent paths, `readlinkat` on `/proc/self/exe`, an `mremap` growth that
preserves contents, and the accepted no-ops.

## Deliberate limits

`poll` reports readiness conservatively and does not distinguish `POLLHUP`;
`epoll` accepts registration but always reports zero events; `sigaltstack` does
not install an alternate stack; `execveat` has no fd-based path; and `clone3`
does not implement `CLONE_VM`, `CLONE_THREAD` or `CLONE_SETTLS`.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone32.md

# Milestone 33: dynamic linking

The kernel now runs dynamically linked Linux executables. `/bin/hello-dyn` is a
musl-linked binary that prints through libc, and the acceptance chain execs it
as its final stage.

## Interpreter loading

`elf::plan` records the `PT_INTERP` segment offset and length.
`exec_runtime::load_static_args` resolves the interpreter path, loads that ELF
from the initramfs, and maps both images before entering:

- The interpreter is mapped at `LD_BASE` (0x4000_0000_0000) and the PIE main
  image above it at `EXE_BASE` (0x4001_0000_0000), both inside the same PML4
  slot. A static `ET_EXEC` keeps its absolute entry-derived slot, so existing
  static binaries are unaffected.
- Entry is the interpreter's entry point (`LD_BASE + interp.entry`); the
  interpreter performs symbol resolution and relocation itself.
- The initial stack carries `AT_BASE` (interpreter base), `AT_PHDR`/`AT_PHENT`/
  `AT_PHNUM` and `AT_ENTRY` describing the main image at its load bias.

The initramfs carries `/lib/libc.so` and `/lib/ld-musl-x86_64.so.1` (the same
musl shared object under both names) plus `/lib` as a directory node.

## RELRO fix

`mprotect` previously returned `EINVAL` when the target range had no VMA
ledger entry. The dynamic linker mprotects its own `PT_GNU_RELRO` range right
after relocation, which lives in the execve image and therefore has no ledger
entry. PTE permission changes still succeed; the ledger update is now treated
as best-effort bookkeeping.

## Bugs fixed along the way

- The private PML4 slot was derived from `max(exe_base, LD_BASE)` for every
  image, which broke static `ET_EXEC` binaries whose segments live in the slot
  implied by their absolute entry point. The slot is now chosen per image kind.
- `mprotect` returning `EINVAL` for ledger-less ranges made every musl
  dynamic binary abort with "RELRO protection failed".

## Deliberate limits

No `dlopen` of paths outside the initramfs, no `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` handling, no
`LD_PRELOAD`, no lazy binding (`RTLD_LAZY` behaves as `RTLD_NOW`), and no
`TLS`-heavy workloads beyond what musl's initial-exec model needs. A
non-PIE dynamically linked binary is not supported.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone33.md

# Milestone 34: threads

`CLONE_VM` threads now run. `/bin/hello-thread` is a musl-linked program that
creates two threads, has them increment a shared counter under a mutex two
hundred times, joins them, and checks the total.

## clone

`sys_clone` accepts the `CLONE_VM` family: `CLONE_VM`, `CLONE_FS`,
`CLONE_FILES`, `CLONE_SIGHAND`, `CLONE_THREAD`, `CLONE_SYSVSEM`,
`CLONE_SETTLS`, `CLONE_PARENT_SETTID`, `CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID`,
`CLONE_CHILD_SETTID` and musl's private `CLONE_DETACHED` (0x400000, which musl
uses instead of `CLONE_CHILD_SETTID`). Anything else is `EINVAL`.
`clone3` dispatches to the same paths based on `CLONE_VM`.

`task::spawn_thread` builds a child that shares the caller's CR3 and
descriptor table (reference-counted clone), runs on the caller-supplied stack
with `fs_base` set from the `CLONE_SETTLS` value, and records the
`CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID` address. The child resumes at the saved syscall return
point with `rax=0`, exactly as a forked child does, so musl's `__clone`
trampoline (`pop arg; call *%r9`) runs unchanged.

## Thread exit

`Task` gained `is_thread`. `task::exit` checks it first: a thread clears its
`clear_child_tid` word, wakes any joiner blocked on that address through the
futex table, and leaves the shared address space and descriptors intact.
Only the last task of a process tears down the image.

## Per-task user RSP

`SYSCALL_USER_RSP` was a single global, which is correct only while one thread
owns the syscall path. With threads, a blocked joiner's user RSP was clobbered
by the thread it was waiting for, and the joiner returned to a garbage stack.
`Task` now carries `user_rsp`; `schedule` parks the outgoing task's value and
restores the incoming task's value around the context switch.

## MAP_STACK

`mmap` rejected `MAP_STACK` (0x20000) and `MAP_GROWSDOWN` (0x100), which
`pthread_create` passes when allocating its stack. Both are accepted and
ignored; only `MAP_FIXED`, `MAP_ANONYMOUS` and the private flag were allowed
before.

## Known issue

Tearing down a process that ran threads prints `image/stack/mmap slot teardown
failed` and `process PML4 recycle failed`. The affected pages are leaked, so a
single acceptance run completes, but the reclaim path needs a follow-up fix.

## Deliberate limits

No `CLONE_SETTLS` validation beyond canonical range, no thread-local
destructors, no `set_robust_list` per-thread registration, no cancellation
points, and no `pthread` affinity or scheduling control. Detached-thread
reaping relies on the joiner futex, matching `CLONE_CHILD_CLEARTID`.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone34.md

FILE: docs/milestone35.md

# Milestone 35: Unix domain sockets

`AF_UNIX` stream sockets work end to end. `/bin/hello-socket` is a musl-linked
program that runs a `socketpair` round trip and a fork-based
`bind`/`listen`/`connect`/`accept` session.

## Socket layer

`kernel/socket.rs` owns a fixed 16-socket table. Each endpoint has a kind, a
state (`Unbound`/`Listening`/`Connected`), an optional bound path, a peer
index and a 4 KiB ring buffer with `readq`/`writeq` wait queues, mirroring the
pipe implementation. `Socket` was added to `OpenKind`, so `read`/`write`,
`pread64`/`pwrite64`, `close` and `fstat` route through it; `seek` reports
`ESPIPE`.

`connect` now establishes the pair immediately: it allocates the server
endpoint, links both sides, and parks `(client, server)` in the listener's
pending queue. `accept` takes the pair and hands the server descriptor over,
so a client can send before the server accepts — matching Linux stream
semantics. Previously the pair was only linked inside `accept`, which made the
client's first `write` fail with `ENOTCONN`.

## Syscalls

`socket`, `bind`, `listen`, `accept`, `connect`, `sendto`, `recvfrom`,
`socketpair`, `shutdown` and `getsockname` are implemented, with
`sockaddr_un` family validation. `read` on a socket returns one `recv`'s
worth instead of looping to the requested length; looping blocked waiting for
bytes the peer never promised.

## fork

musl's `fork` calls `SYS_fork` (57), not `clone`; the dispatcher previously
only handled `clone`, so `fork` returned `ENOSYS` and every musl program that
forked failed. `sys_fork` now shares the `fork_process` path.

## Deliberate limits

`SOCK_DGRAM` is accepted but behaves like a stream, `SO_PEERCRED` and
`SO_SNDBUF` options are unimplemented, there is no `sendmsg`/`recvmsg` scatter
support, socket files are not visible in the VFS, and `accept` always blocks.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone35.md

FILE: docs/milestone36.md

# Milestone 36: virtio-net and TCP/IP

`AF_INET` sockets work end to end over QEMU user networking. `/bin/hello-net`,
chained after the Unix-socket test, runs a UDP `sendto`/`recvfrom` echo and a
TCP `connect`/`write`/`read` echo against a host server on `10.0.2.2:5555`.

## PCI and virtio-net

`kernel/arch/x86_64/port.rs` adds port-I/O primitives. `kernel/driver/pci.rs`
now scans the CAM (`0xcf8`/`0xcfc`), handles multi-function devices and
64-bit BARs, and enables memory/IO decoding. `kernel/driver/virtio_net.rs` is
a legacy (0.9.5) virtio-net driver for `1af4:1000`: it negotiates no features,
reads the MAC from config space, and uses 256-entry split rings (descriptors at
offset 0, available ring at 4096, used ring at 8192; 32 RX buffers and 8 TX
buffers behind a single lock).

Two legacy quirks cost the debugging time:

- The available-ring helpers must point at offset 4096, after the 4096-byte
  descriptor table. Pointing at offset 2 corrupts descriptor 0 and makes the
  device see an available index of zero forever.
- With no negotiated features every buffer carries the 10-byte
  `virtio_net_hdr` prefix on both TX and RX, so the driver zeroes it before
  frames and skips it after them. Without the prefix QEMU parses the frame's
  first bytes as a header and slirp drops the garbage.

## Stack

`kernel/net.rs` implements Ethernet, ARP (4-entry cache, replies to requests
for our IP), IPv4 (identification, DF, checksum), ICMP echo, UDP with
per-socket queues, and a minimal TCP client (go-back-N with RTO and a
retransmit budget, window shrinking on receive, FIN/`shutdown` handling).
Transmission is synchronous: the driver spins the TX used ring after each
notification, which QEMU completes synchronously for a PIO kick.

One checksum subtlety: the one's-complement fold of a *valid* packet is `0`,
not `0xffff`, because the helper returns the complement. Verifying with
`!= 0xffff` rejects every correct datagram.

## Delivery model

There are no device interrupts. The APIC timer handler calls `net::poll()`
in addition to the scheduler tick, and blocking waits (`resolve`, `udp_recv`,
TCP handshake) loop `poll()` plus a nap that ends in `sti;hlt;cli` every
eighth round. Syscalls run with interrupts disabled (`FMASK` clears `IF`), so
the inline polling is what keeps these waits live; the tick polls too, so data
that arrives during a nap is dispatched by the time the wait resumes.

## Sockets and ABI

`socket.rs` dispatches ids `16..24` to UDP and `32..40` to TCP via
`inet_kind()`; the Linux ABI layer parses `sockaddr_in` into `(ip, port)`
pairs and writes them back for `getsockname`/`getpeername` (nr 52).
`sendto`/`recvfrom` work with and without addresses, `bind` supports
`INADDR_ANY` and specific addresses, and `shutdown` forwards its `how` for
TCP. `listen`/`accept` on inet sockets report `EOPNOTSUPP`; server-side TCP is
future work.

## Testing

`tools/net-echo.py` binds UDP and TCP on `127.0.0.1:5555` (slirp rewrites
`10.0.2.2`), writes `build/net-echo.ready`, and the acceptance script starts
it, waits for readiness, and kills it on exit. Markers cover the exec into
`/bin/hello-net`, `net: main entered`, and the passing UDP/TCP echo.

## Deliberate limits

No DHCP (static `10.0.2.15`/`10.0.2.2`), no ICMP errors, no fragmentation
(MSS 1400, MTU 1500 with `DF`), no server-side `listen`/`accept`, no
`IP_FREEBIND`-style options, single outstanding segment window stretch, and a
small fixed socket table (8 UDP, 8 TCP).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone36.md

SHA-256: e78341f91f32a9c55914e8be266ac693f1de5dde59ef7ec41b4e0a144febf8e3
================================================================================
# Milestone 4: executable image and VMA semantics

This milestone moves compatibility from isolated syscall stubs toward a coherent Linux process image.

## ELF

The parser reads little-endian fields from bytes instead of casting an arbitrary buffer to an aligned Rust reference. It accepts x86-64 `ET_EXEC` and `ET_DYN`, validates the complete program-header table, plans up to 16 `PT_LOAD` segments, rejects overflow/truncation, verifies alignment congruence, records `PT_INTERP`, and requires the entry point to fall inside the image.

## Virtual memory

A fixed-capacity, allocation-free VMA ledger tracks sorted non-overlapping user ranges and Linux protection bits. Anonymous private mappings can now reserve addresses through `mmap`; exact mappings can be changed or removed through `mprotect` and `munmap`.

This is intentionally the policy layer only. A returned mapping is not usable until the architecture page-table backend installs PTEs and page-fault handling allocates/zeros frames. The next milestone must connect every ledger mutation transactionally to hardware page tables before Linux binaries execute.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone4.md

================================================================================
# Milestone 5: Linux process image construction

This milestone constructs the data required immediately before entering a Linux x86-64 executable.

The execution planner turns validated ELF `PT_LOAD` records into page-aligned mapping operations, derives Linux protection flags, locates the in-memory program-header table for `AT_PHDR`, reserves a 1 MiB user stack, and records whether `PT_INTERP` requires a dynamic linker.

The stack builder creates `argc`, NULL-terminated `argv`, NULL-terminated `envp`, and the auxiliary vector. It copies all strings, places 16 random bytes, includes `AT_EXECFN`, and returns a 16-byte-aligned initial RSP. It rejects embedded NUL bytes and oversized input without partially writing outside the supplied stack buffer.

Remaining integration work is deliberately separate: install PTEs, copy segment bytes, zero BSS, commit the stack page, set FS state, and transition to ring 3. Dynamic binaries remain blocked until the interpreter and file-backed mmap path exist.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone5.md

# Milestone 55: Resource accounting and VFS mount namespaces

Moved mount routing from the Linux syscall layer into the VFS. Mount selection
uses the longest matching mountpoint in the caller's mount namespace. Added
fixed-capacity tmpfs stores, tmpfs file/directory operations, ext2 mount routing,
and mount-table cloning for `CLONE_NEWNS`, `fork`, and `unshare`. `mount` and
`umount2` now use the VFS registry and require `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` for the current
mount namespace. Unmounting a
mount with nested mounts returns `EBUSY`.

Added descriptor/open-file counts, VM area/byte/page statistics, and scheduler
state counts. Process exit reports resources before and after teardown, and the
acceptance test rejects incomplete address-space cleanup. The physical-frame
recycle pool now holds 65,536 pages, covering the configured 256 MiB guest; this
removes the previous 256-page teardown bottleneck.

Implemented reachable `capget`/`capset` handlers, capability checks for identity
changes and privileged namespace operations, and `PR_SET/GET_NO_NEW_PRIVS`.
Seccomp now validates supported classic-BPF instructions, rejects unsupported
flags and replacement filters, shares filter lifetime safely across forked
tasks, and returns errno/trap/kill/allow actions without treating trace and user
notification as allow.

Inotify now matches events to the correct watch descriptor, queues complete
records, reports overflow and ignored-watch events, removes state on final fd
close, and delivers create/modify events. The io_uring subset validates setup
parameters and ring indices, uses kernel-maintained SQ/CQ cursors, resolves SQE
file descriptors through the process fd table, checks user buffers, and tracks
shared ring mappings. The differential test submits a NOP and checks its CQE.

The differential test checks representative `ENOENT`, `EBADF`, and `EINVAL`
results, reads `/proc/self/status`, exercises capget/capset, seccomp, inotify and
io_uring, verifies QEMU's success exit status, and restores temporarily modified
inputs even when the run fails.

## Limits

- Mount and tmpfs tables are fixed-capacity; tmpfs file storage is limited to
  eight pages per file. Tmpfs hard links are not implemented.
- Tmpfs backing stores are retained after unmount so open inode handles remain
  valid; reclaiming an unreferenced store is future work.
- Mount flags and `mount(2)` data are not interpreted. Supported filesystem
  types are tmpfs and ext2; ext2 remains read-only.
- Seccomp supports a validated classic-BPF subset and one filter per process;
  unsupported filter actions and synchronization flags are rejected or fail
  closed. Inotify implements a subset of event sources and does not block on
  empty reads.
- io_uring supports NOP, READ and WRITE with rings up to 64 entries; registration,
  nonzero enter flags, and minimum-completion waiting are unsupported.
- Resource diagnostics cover process descriptors, VM ledger entries, and task
  slots; they are not a complete global allocator/leak detector.

## Verification

- `./tools/check-source.sh` — passed.
- `./tools/differential-test.sh` — passed for native and guest runs.
- `OVMF_CODE=/usr/share/OVMF/OVMF_CODE_4M.fd OVMF_VARS_TEMPLATE=/usr/share/OVMF/OVMF_VARS_4M.fd TIMEOUT=40 ./tools/acceptance-test.sh` — passed with no incomplete-cleanup markers.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone55.md

# Milestone 56: Memory completeness

## Goal
Make the virtual-memory subsystem production-capable: demand-paged anonymous and
file-backed mappings, copy-on-write fork, lazy brk, correct unmap/teardown under
sharing, and user-mode page-fault recovery. Target: musl-linked binaries with
`malloc`/`fork`/`exec` heavy workloads run unmodified with zero frame leaks.

## Scope
- Anonymous mmap becomes lazy (reserve VMA, allocate on first touch).
- File-backed mmap (read) becomes lazy and shareable; private writes use COW.
- `fork` shares pages read-only with reference counts instead of deep-copying.
- Write faults to a shared anonymous page allocate a private copy.
- `brk` growth reserves pages; faults allocate zeroed frames.
- `mremap(MREMAP_MAYMOVE|FIXED)`, `mlock`/`munlock`, `mincore`.
- User-mode `#PF` handler resolves faults against the VMA ledger before
  terminating the process.

Out of scope (M60): writable shared file-backed mappings, page cache write-back,
`msync`, `madvise` beyond hints.

## Data structures

### `memory.rs` — frame reference counts
Add a fixed-capacity refcount table sized to the configured guest (65536 frames
for 256 MiB). Frames are indexed by `pa / PAGE_SIZE`.

```rust
static REFCOUNTS: SpinLock<[u16; MAX_FRAMES]> = ...;
pub fn refcount(pa: u64) -> u16;
pub fn refcount_inc(pa: u64);
/// Decrements; returns true if the caller must free the frame.
pub fn refcount_dec(pa: u64) -> bool;
```

`alloc_frame` initializes the slot to 1. `free_frame` is unchanged for
non-shared (kernel) callers; user paths use `release_frame` which decrements and
frees only at zero. External (io_uring) mappings keep their own refcount and are
untouched.

### `vm.rs` — VMA kind and metadata
Extend `Area`:

```rust
pub enum VmKind { Anon, File { ino: u64, offset: u64 }, Stack, Brk }
pub struct Area {
    pub start: usize, pub end: usize,
    pub flags: VmFlags,
    pub kind: VmKind,
    pub cow: bool,        // read-only shared, copy on write
}
```

`Map` gains `find_vma(addr) -> Option<Area>` (already derivable from
`flags_at`/iteration) and `set_cow(start, end, bool)`.

### `process.rs`
- `map_zeroed` is removed from the eager path; `mmap` reserves the VMA only.
- New `handle_page_fault(addr: u64, write: bool) -> Result<(), ()>`:
  1. Look up VMA containing `addr`. Missing => return Err (caller delivers
     SIGSEGV).
  2. If present PTE already maps the page:
     - write fault on a COW VMA => allocate copy, map writable, decrement old.
     - otherwise => Err.
  3. If no PTE:
     - Anon/Brk/Stack => allocate zeroed frame, map with VMA prot.
     - File => allocate frame, read file page (respecting EOF -> zero), map
       read-only (or per prot for private).
     - If VMA is COW-shared, map read-only and set cow.
  4. Update TLB via invlpg (map_page already does).
- `clone_address_space` (fork): instead of `copy_slot` deep copy, mark both
  parent and child pages read-only, set `cow=true` on shared VMAs, increment
  frame refcounts. The PML4 slots are cloned at the table level (shallow) with
  pages shared.
- `teardown_exit`/`unmap_pages`/`rollback` use `release_frame` for non-external
  pages so shared frames survive until last unmap.
- `brk`: reserve `[old_page, new_page)` in the VMA ledger (kind=Brk) without
  allocating; faults fill.

## Page-fault path
`arch/x86_64/idt.rs` (or exceptions.S trampoline) already routes `#PF` to
`exit_fault`. Change the user-mode branch:

```
if user_mode {
    if process::handle_page_fault(cr2, err_code & 2 != 0).is_ok() { return; } // iretq
    task::exit_fault(vector, err, rip, cr2);
}
```

Kernel-mode faults remain fatal (no recovery this milestone). `usercopy` faults
are still unrecoverable and stay fatal; exception-table recovery is M63.

## COW details
- On fork, after shallow-cloning page tables, walk each shared slot and clear
  `WRITABLE` on every present leaf PTE in both parent and child, setting the
  software COW marker. Linux uses a software bit in the PTE; here we use the
  VMA `cow` flag plus read-only PTEs (sufficient because VMA lookup decides).
- Write fault: if PTE present and read-only and VMA.cow:
  - if refcount(pa) == 1: just re-map writable (no copy needed).
  - else: alloc frame, copy 4 KiB, dec old refcount, map new frame writable,
    set VMA.cow=false for that page range (split VMA if needed — for simplicity,
    split the VMA at fault page boundaries on first COW write).
- VMA splitting: implement `Map::split(start, len)` to carve a non-COW sub-range
  so accounting stays accurate. If splitting exhausts `MAX_AREAS`, fall back to
  marking the whole VMA non-COW (acceptable, slightly over-approximates).

## Demand paging for anonymous mmap
- `mmap` (anon): validate, insert VMA with kind=Anon, flags from prot, cow=false.
  No frame allocation. Return start.
- First access faults -> `handle_page_fault` allocates.
- `MAP_POPULATE`: eager `map_zeroed` (keep for compatibility).
- `munmap`: walk pages, unmap + release_frame each present PTE, remove VMA.

## File-backed mmap (read, private)
- VMA kind=File{ino, offset}. Fault reads the page from the inode into a fresh
  frame. For tmpfs, use `vfs::read_off` into the frame; for ext2, `ext2::read`.
- MAP_PRIVATE writes: mark VMA cow=true, PTE read-only; first write copies.
- MAP_SHARED writes: deferred to M60 (return ENODEV for writable shared file
  mappings, as today).

## brk
- `brk` reserves VMA range kind=Brk; faults allocate zeroed frames. Shrink
  (`new_page < old_page`) remains unsupported (return old), matching current
  behavior, to avoid complex unmap of partial ranges.

## mremap
- Existing implementation copies bytes and frees old. Keep, but update to use
  `release_frame` and to preserve VMA kind. Add `MREMAP_FIXED`: unmap target
  range first (release frames), then move.
- If old range is COW-shared, materialize copies before moving (current copy
  loop already does).

## mlock / munlock / mincore
- `mlock`: set a `locked` bit on the VMA; no reclaim exists, so correctness is
  tracking only. `RLIMIT_MEMLOCK` check (stub: allow).
- `munlock`: clear bit.
- `mincore`: for each page in range, report resident if a PTE is present
  (all mapped pages are resident in this model).

## Acceptance criteria
1. `build-uefi.sh` still produces a bootable image; acceptance test passes.
2. Differential test passes (host + guest) including new mmap/fork tests added to
   `tests/differential.c` (mmap lazy touch, fork COW write, brk growth, mremap).
3. New self-tests:
   - `vm::cow_self_test()` in boot: fork a process, write to a shared page in
     child, verify parent value unchanged and frame counts balance.
   - `memory::refcount_self_test()`.
4. Soak: run `/bin/differential` in a loop 1000 times under QEMU; no
   "cleanup incomplete" markers and stable frame count.

## Implementation order (within M56)
1. `memory.rs` refcount API + self-test.
2. `vm.rs` Area extension + `find_vma` + self-test update.
3. `process.rs` `handle_page_fault` + lazy anonymous mmap + lazy brk.
4. Wire `#PF` user branch in `idt.rs`.
5. COW fork: shallow clone + refcount + read-only PTEs.
6. File-backed lazy mmap (read).
7. `mremap` FIXED, `mlock`/`munlock`/`mincore` syscalls.
8. Tests + differential updates.

## Risks
- Lazy mmap changes observable timing; programs that rely on eager OOM (touch
  all pages) still fault on access, preserving semantics.
- COW VMA splitting may exhaust `MAX_AREAS` (64); if so, keep whole-VMA COW
  and accept slight over-sharing (correct but less granular).
- Frame refcount table must cover io_uring external frames without double
  counting; external frames are never in refcount table (they use their own
  `IOURING_MAP_REFS`).
- Kernel-mode faults remain fatal; `usercopy` recovery deferred to M63.

## Verification
- `./tools/check-source.sh` and `./tools/check-toolchain.sh` pass.
- `cargo +nightly build -Zbuild-std=core --release` clean.
- `./tools/build-uefi.sh` succeeds.
- `OVMF_CODE=... OVMF_VARS_TEMPLATE=... ./tools/acceptance-test.sh` prints
  `acceptance: PASS` with no incomplete-cleanup markers.
- `./tools/differential-test.sh` prints `differential test: PASS`.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
END FILE: docs/milestone56.md

FILE: docs/milestone57.md

# Milestone 57: procfs truth and process introspection

## Goal
Replace the placeholder `/proc` with real per-process and system data so that
process-inspection tools and musl programs see truthful state: actual command
lines, environment, memory maps, residency, scheduler identity, and descriptor
link targets. Target: `/proc/self/*`, `/proc/<pid>/*` and the system files parse
identically to Linux for the fields Slop/MNU implements.

## Scope
- `/proc/self/{cmdline,environ,status,stat,statm,maps}` from live process state.
- `/proc/<pid>/{cmdline,environ,status,stat,statm,maps,exe,root,cwd}` for live
  processes (no threads-as-processes; `Tgid`/`Threads` reflect CLONE_THREAD).
- `/proc/self/{fd,ns}` directory enumeration and dynamic `/proc/self/fd/N`
  readlink targets (`pipe:[N]`, `socket:[N]`, `anon_inode:[...]`, paths).
- System files: `/proc/{meminfo,uptime,version,cpuinfo,loadavg,mounts}` from
  frame allocator, monotonic clock, scheduler and mount registry.
- `readlink`/`readlinkat` route through the VFS for all `/proc` symlinks
  (previously only `/proc/self/exe` in `readlinkat`).
- `madvise(MADV_DONTNEED|MADV_FREE)` discards anonymous pages; `MAP_POPULATE`
  eagerly faults mappings; `mincore` reports true PTE residency.

Out of scope (later milestones): `/proc/<pid>/fd`, smaps, task subdirectories,
per-thread stat files, writable proc files, sysctl.

## Data model

### `exec_runtime.rs` — recorded exec metadata
`Loaded` carries what `/proc` must report:
```rust
pub struct MapSeg { pub start: u64, pub end: u64, pub flags: u32 } // PT_LOAD perms
pub cmdline: [u8; 512], cmdline_len: usize,   // argv packed with NULs
pub environ: [u8; 512], environ_len: usize,   // envp packed with NULs
pub segs: [MapSeg; MAX_MAP_SEGS], nsegs: usize,
pub stack_base: u64, stack_top: u64,
```
`load_static_args` records interpreter and main-object `PT_LOAD` extents plus
the initial stack bounds and packs `argv`/`envp` via `pack_vec`.

### `process.rs`
- New fields mirroring the above; `fork_child` inherits them unchanged.
- `set_exec_meta(&Loaded)` stores exec metadata on `execve`.
- `vma_bytes()` sums VMA extents; `resident_pages()` walks the page table and
  counts present user leaves.
- `madvise(addr, len, advice)`: `DONTNEED`/`FREE` unmap present pages inside
  valid VMAs (ENOMEM outside), other advice returns success. Pages re-fault as
  zero-filled, matching anonymous DONTNEED semantics.
- `mmap`: allow `MAP_POPULATE`; after inserting the VMA, fault every page in via
  `handle_page_fault` (rollback on failure), so `mincore` reports residency.

### `task.rs`
- `with_process_pid(pid, f)`: read-only view of another live process.
- `live_pids(cb)`, `ppid_of(pid)`, `thread_count(pid)`, `last_pid()` for
  `/proc` enumeration, `PPid`, `Threads`, and `loadavg`.

### `vfs.rs` — proc routing
- `parse_proc(path) -> Option<(ProcSubject, &str)>` recognizes `/proc/self`
  and numeric PIDs; `proc_with` resolves either subject to a `Process`.
- `proc_fill(subject, file)` renders cmdline/environ/status/stat/statm/maps and
  the system files into `PROC_BUF` (capacity 4096, Linux-style truncation).
  - `maps`: `PT_LOAD` segments with `rwxp` perms from ELF flags, VMAs with
    `rwxp` from `VmFlags` and `[heap]`/`[stack]` labels, file mappings with
    offset and inode number.
  - `stat`: pid/comm/state/ppid/pgrp/session, utime/stime `0`, priority `20`,
    `Threads`, `VmSize` (bytes), `VmResident` (pages), remaining fields zero.
  - `status`: `Name` (basename of `exe_path`), `State`, `Tgid`/`Pid`/`PPid`,
    `Uid`/`Gid` quadruples, `Threads`, `VmSize`/`VmRSS` in kB.
  - `meminfo` from `memory::frame_stats()` (total/free frames, 4 KiB frames).
  - `uptime` from `time::monotonic_ns()` as `seconds.centiseconds`.
  - `loadavg` from live PID count and `last_pid`; `mounts` from the mount
    registry filtered by the caller's mount namespace.
- `readdir` enumerates `/proc` (self + live PIDs + system files) and
  `/proc/self/fd` dynamically from `FdTable::open_fds`; `/proc/<pid>` lists the
  per-process files and links.
- `/proc/self/fd/N` readlink targets are derived from `fd::kind`: console path,
  inode path, `pipe:[N]`, `socket:[N]`, and `anon_inode:[...]` forms.
- `proc_symlink` materializes link targets in a frame; generic
  `sys_readlink`/`sys_readlinkat` now serve every `/proc` link.

### ABI
- `madvise` dispatch wired to `Process::madvise`.
- `mincore` validates alignment, ENOMEM for unmapped ranges, then reports
  present leaves per page into the user vector.
- `MAP_POPULATE` accepted in the mmap flag mask.

## Acceptance criteria
1. `./tools/acceptance-test.sh` prints `acceptance: PASS` with all resource
   reclamation markers intact.
2. `./tools/differential-test.sh` prints `differential test: PASS`, including
   new host/guest parity cases:
   - `DIFF_PROCFS_OK`: status/cmdline/stat/pid-path/maps/uptime/meminfo shapes
     and `fd/1` readlink format.
   - `DIFF_MADVISE_OK`: `MADV_DONTNEED` zeroes anonymous pages on next read.
   - `DIFF_MINCORE_OK`: residency bits for touched vs untouched pages, and
     `MAP_POPULATE` makes a fresh mapping fully resident.
3. `./tools/check-source.sh` passes.

## Verification
- `cargo +nightly build -Zbuild-std=core --release` clean.
- `OVMF_CODE=... OVMF_VARS_TEMPLATE=... ./tools/acceptance-test.sh` PASS.
- `./tools/differential-test.sh` PASS (native Linux vs QEMU guest).
- `./tools/check-source.sh` pass.

## Risks
- `/proc` files are rendered at lookup into a single shared buffer; a second
  lookup between `open` and `read` replaces the contents. Slop/MNU's inode copy
  keeps `size` and page pointers, so readers still see a consistent snapshot
  only if they open+read immediately; acceptable for this milestone.
- `PROC_BUF` is 4096 bytes; large maps lists truncate as on Linux.
- `proc_symlink` allocates a frame per lookup and those frames are not yet
  reclaimed; deferring reclaim keeps the link path allocation-free at read
  time and is bounded by lookup frequency during this milestone.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Milestone 58: nonblocking I/O, fcntl status flags, FD_CLOEXEC, flock

## Goal
Make descriptor status flags behave like Linux: `O_NONBLOCK` on opens, pipes
and sockets, `O_APPEND` at write time, `F_GETFL`/`F_SETFL` round trips,
`F_GETFD`/`F_SETFD` close-on-exec, and `flock(2)` advisory locking. Programs
that set descriptors nonblocking and poll them (or rely on `flock`) must see
the same behavior on the host and in the guest.

## Scope
- Per-open-file status flags: `O_APPEND`, `O_NONBLOCK`, `FD_CLOEXEC`.
- `fcntl`: `F_DUPFD`, `F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC`, `F_GETFD`, `F_SETFD`, `F_GETFL`,
  `F_SETFL`, `F_GETLK` (always reports `F_UNLCK` — no POSIX record locks).
- `open`/`openat`: `O_APPEND`, `O_NONBLOCK`, `O_CLOEXEC`, `O_TRUNC`.
- `socket`/`accept4`: `SOCK_NONBLOCK`, `SOCK_CLOEXEC`.
- `dup3` with `O_CLOEXEC`; `execve` closes `FD_CLOEXEC` descriptors.
- `flock(2)`: `LOCK_SH`/`LOCK_EX`/`LOCK_NB`/`LOCK_UN`, blocking and
  nonblocking contention via the existing wait-queue machinery.
- `sendto`/`recvfrom`/`sendmsg`/`recvmsg`: `MSG_DONTWAIT`.
- Kernel stack hardening discovered while testing: 64 KiB kernel stacks and a
  syscall-entry overflow guard.

Out of scope (later milestones): POSIX record locks (`F_SETLK`/`F_SETLKW`),
`O_ASYNC`, `F_SETOWN`, file leases, `flock` across `fork` inheritance nuance
beyond per-open-file sharing.

## Data model

### `fd.rs` — open-file status flags
- `OpenFile` gains `flags: u32` (`O_APPEND|O_NONBLOCK`, `STATUS_MASK`), shared
  by reference: `dup`/`fork` copy the flags through the shared `OpenFile`, and
  `F_SETFL` on one fd is visible through all aliases, as on Linux.
- `FdTable` gains a parallel `close_exec` table; `close_on_exec()` clears both
  the slot and the cached `OpenFile` reference of `FD_CLOEXEC` descriptors.
- `flags(id)`, `flags_set(id, set, clear)`, `is_nonblock(id)` centralize flag
  updates; `F_SETFL` replaces the `O_APPEND|O_NONBLOCK` bits (set and clear in
  one call).
- `alloc_file_flags`, `open_flags`, `create_open_flags`, and `pipe2` carry the
  flag through creation.
- `write` with `O_APPEND` resolves the start offset through
  `vfs::inode_size(ino)` instead of the cached inode snapshot, and `seek`
  `SEEK_END` does the same, so appends land at the live end of file.

### `flock.rs` — advisory locks
- Fixed 32-entry table keyed by `(fd-identity, inode)`; `LOCK_SH`/`LOCK_EX`
  replace the caller's previous lock then scan for conflicts (other
  open-file's) locks, so upgrade/downgrade is not deadlock-prone.
- Contending blocking calls register on a per-entry wait queue and park; the
  holder's `release` wakes all waiters. Nonblocking (`LOCK_NB`) contention
  returns `EWOULDBLOCK`.
- Non-inode kinds (console, eventfd, ...) accept the call and report success
  without state; char/block devices use a pseudo-inode so `flock` on
  `/dev/...` nodes still works.

### `vfs.rs`
- `inode_size(ino) -> Option<u64>` looks up the live size for tmpfs and root
  inodes; used by append writes and `SEEK_END`.

### `linux_abi`
- `open_common(p, flags, mode)` is shared by `sys_open` and `sys_openat`
  (musl's `open()` issues syscall 2, so both paths must honor the full flag
  set, including `O_TRUNC` through `vfs::truncate_path`).
- `accept_impl(fd, addr, addrlen, extra)` implements `accept` and `accept4`;
  the accepted descriptor inherits the listener's nonblocking mode and applies
  the `accept4` flags (`SOCK_CLOEXEC`/`SOCK_NONBLOCK`).
- `sys_socket` honors `SOCK_NONBLOCK`/`SOCK_CLOEXEC` in the type argument.
- `sys_dup3` applies `O_CLOEXEC` to the new descriptor.
- `sys_flock` wired to the flock module.
- Read/write socket paths treat a descriptor as nonblocking when either the
  open file has `O_NONBLOCK` or the call passes `MSG_DONTWAIT`.

### `task.rs` — kernel stack hardening
- `STACK_SIZE` grows to 64 KiB. While validating the new tests, the
  fork→`execve`→`wait` path was found to overflow the forked child's 32 KiB
  kernel stack slot into the adjacent slot (the parent's), corrupting the
  parent's saved `wait4` frame. Worst-case exec-path usage measured ~28 KiB
  plus untracked deeper frames; 64 KiB restores headroom without changing the
  boot-time stack.
- `stack_guard()` runs at every syscall entry and halts with a diagnostic if
  the stack pointer leaves the task's own slot, turning silent corruption into
  a deterministic failure.

## Acceptance criteria
1. `./tools/acceptance-test.sh` prints `acceptance: PASS` with all resource
   reclamation markers intact.
2. `./tools/differential-test.sh` prints `differential test: PASS`, including
   new host/guest parity cases:
   - `DIFF_FLOCK_OK`: `LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB`/`LOCK_SH|LOCK_NB` contention reports
     `EWOULDBLOCK`, unlock releases, and a shared lock is granted after the
     exclusive holder exits.
   - `DIFF_NONBLOCK_OK`: `pipe2(O_NONBLOCK)` read returns `EAGAIN`;
     `F_GETFL` shows `O_NONBLOCK`; `F_SETFL` clears it;
     `F_GETFD`/`F_SETFD` round trip `FD_CLOEXEC`.
   - `DIFF_APPEND_OK`: `O_APPEND` writes land at end after `lseek` back.
   - `DIFF_CLOEXEC_OK`: a `FD_CLOEXEC` descriptor is gone after `execve`
     (child re-execs itself and reports `EBADF`), exercising fork → exec →
     wait end to end.
3. `./tools/check-source.sh` passes.

## Verification
- `cargo +nightly build -Zbuild-std=core --release` clean.
- `OVMF_CODE=... OVMF_VARS_TEMPLATE=... ./tools/acceptance-test.sh` PASS.
- `./tools/differential-test.sh` PASS (native Linux vs QEMU guest).
- `./tools/check-source.sh` pass.

## Risks
- `flock` is a fixed 32-entry table; exhaustion returns `ENOMEM`.
- `F_GETLK` reports `F_UNLCK` unconditionally: no POSIX record locks exist, so
  programs mixing `fcntl` locks with `flock` see locks they set through
  `fcntl` as absent. Acceptable until record locks are implemented.
- `F_SETFL` only tracks `O_APPEND`/`O_NONBLOCK`; other status bits are
  ignored rather than rejected.
- `F_GETFL` reports `O_RDWR` as the access mode regardless of how the
  descriptor was opened; musl callers mask the access mode, so this is only
  visible through raw `fcntl`.
- 64 KiB per task × 8 tasks grows the static kernel BSS by 256 KiB; the EFI
  loader image stays well within the QEMU memory budget.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Milestone 59: POSIX timers, clock_settime and robust-list round trip

## Goal
Give processes real interval timers and a settable wall clock: the
`timer_create` family delivering signals with `si_value`/`si_overrun`,
`alarm`/`setitimer` sharing the same per-process ITIMER_REAL, a
`CLOCK_REALTIME` seeded from the platform RTC and adjustable with
`clock_settime`, and `set/get_robust_list` round-tripping. While wiring the
timer tick, the timer IRQ stub's stack alignment was found to be
statically assumed; it is now dynamic.

## Scope
- `timer_create` (SIGEV_SIGNAL, SIGEV_NONE, SIGEV_THREAD_ID),
  `timer_settime` (incl. `TIMER_ABSTIME`), `timer_gettime`,
  `timer_getoverrun`, `timer_delete`.
- `alarm`, `getitimer`/`setitimer` for `ITIMER_REAL` (shared per-process
  timer; `ITIMER_VIRTUAL`/`ITIMER_PROF` report `EINVAL` — no CPU-time
  accounting yet).
- `clock_settime(CLOCK_REALTIME)` and truthful `clock_gettime`/
  `clock_getres` classification: realtime, monotonic, boottime, CPU clocks
  as zero, coarse variants.
- `clock_nanosleep` honors the clock id and `TIMER_ABSTIME`.
- RTC seeding of `CLOCK_REALTIME` at boot (binary and BCD modes, 12h/24h).
- `set_robust_list`/`get_robust_list` storage and round trip.
- `rt_sigpending` and `rt_sigtimedwait` (zero and blocking timeouts);
  `pause` returns `EINTR` for deliverable signals.
- Timer IRQ entry: dynamic 16-byte stack alignment before the Rust handler.

Out of scope: robust-futex `EOWNERDEAD`/`FUTEX_OWNER_DIED` processing on
thread exit (round trip only), `SIGEV_THREAD` at the kernel level (musl
translates it to `SIGEV_THREAD_ID|SIGEV_SIGNAL` in userspace),
`ITIMER_VIRTUAL`/`ITIMER_PROF`, CPU-time clocks, POSIX record locks.

## Data model

### `posix_timer.rs` — interval timers and alarms
- 32-entry POSIX timer table keyed by a monotonic id, plus an 8-entry
  per-PID alarm slot table (one live process each). Both are plain
  `SpinLock`s; the tick hook only mutates the tables under their locks and
  queues signals after dropping them, so no scheduler lock is taken while
  the timer table is held.
- `Notify::Signal{sig,value}` / `Notify::None` model `sigevent`.
  `SIGEV_THREAD` (2) is rejected as on Linux; `SIGEV_THREAD_ID` (4) is
  accepted and delivered process-wide (no tid targeting).
- Expiry accounting: a tick that finds `next <= now` computes the full
  number of missed expirations, folds one delivery into a signal and
  records the rest as overrun. When the signal is already pending the
  count accumulates in the timer entry; `timer_getoverrun` reports it.
- `alarm_set`/`alarm_get` implement the per-process ITIMER_REAL with
  remaining/interval in ticks; `alarm()` is the seconds-only view.

### `task.rs` / `process.rs` — delivery and siginfo storage
- `timer_signal(pid, sig, value, overrun)` queues the bit under the
  process lock, records `si_code = SI_TIMER` plus value/overrun in new
  per-process arrays, and wakes the owner; it returns whether the signal
  coalesced so the timer table can account overrun.
- `Process` gains `si_code`/`si_value`/`si_overrun` arrays (reset at exec)
  and `robust_list_head`/`robust_list_len`.
- `on_timer_tick` now calls `posix_timer::tick(now)` after waking sleepers.

### `linux_abi` — syscall surface
- `rt_sigtimedwait` writes a user-layout `siginfo_t` (timer: `si_timerid`
  @16, `si_overrun` @20, `si_value` @24, matching the host kernel for both
  musl and glibc callers), returning the signal number or `EAGAIN`.
- `rt_sigpending` copies the pending mask; `pause` returns `EINTR` when a
  deliverable signal (handler or terminating default) is pending.
- `clock_gettime/getres` classify clock ids; `CLOCK_REALTIME` is
  `monotonic + offset`, the offset seeded once from the CMOS RTC and
  replaced by `clock_settime`. `clock_nanosleep` supports `TIMER_ABSTIME`.
- `execve` deletes the process's POSIX timers (POSIX: timers are not
  preserved across exec).

### `arch/x86_64` — RTC and IRQ alignment
- `rtc.rs` reads the CMOS clock: BCD/binary status, 12h PM translation,
  century register with a year-based fallback, sanity bounds, and a
  stable-read check. Failure to read leaves the offset at zero.
- `irq.S`: the timer stub aligned the stack with a fixed `sub $8`, which
  only holds when the interrupted kernel context has a particular
  `rsp % 16`. A `movaps` in the new tick code faulted with #GP on the
  first tick. The stub now saves `rsp` in `r15`, masks to 16 bytes before
  the call, and restores afterwards — the same pattern `exceptions.S`
  already used.

## Acceptance criteria
1. `./tools/acceptance-test.sh` prints `acceptance: PASS` with all resource
   reclamation markers intact.
2. `./tools/differential-test.sh` prints `differential test: PASS`,
   including new host/guest parity cases:
   - `DIFF_TIMER_OK`: one-shot timer signal arrives, `si_code == SI_TIMER`,
     `si_value` round-trips, overrun is readable, the timer disarms and
     `timer_delete` invalidates the id, unknown clocks are rejected.
   - `DIFF_ITIMER_OK`: `setitimer(ITIMER_REAL)` fires `SIGALRM`, `getitimer`
     reads zero after expiry, `alarm(5)` then `alarm(0)` returns 5.
   - `DIFF_CLOCK_OK`: realtime resolution/shape, `EINVAL` for unknown
     clocks, monotonic `clock_settime` rejected.
   - `DIFF_ROBUST_OK`: `get_robust_list` reports length 24, `set` then
     `get` round-trips the head pointer, bad length is `EINVAL`.
   - `DIFF_SIGPEND_OK`: raised blocked signal appears in `sigpending`, a
     zero-timeout `sigtimedwait` consumes it, the next one is `EAGAIN`.
3. `./tools/check-source.sh` passes.

## Verification
- `cargo +nightly build -Zbuild-std=core --release` clean.
- `OVMF_CODE=... OVMF_VARS_TEMPLATE=... ./tools/acceptance-test.sh` PASS.
- `./tools/differential-test.sh` PASS (native Linux vs QEMU guest).
- `./tools/check-source.sh` pass.

## Risks
- Signals remain a process-wide bitmask: coalesced timer signals collapse
  to one bit, so `si_value` for a later timer sharing an already-pending
  signal is not distinguishable; per-timer overrun counting compensates in
  the common single-timer case.
- The POSIX timer table is a fixed 32 entries (`EAGAIN` when exhausted).
- `ITIMER_VIRTUAL`/`ITIMER_PROF` return `EINVAL` instead of counting CPU
  time; programs that profile themselves will fail fast rather than hang.
- RTC reads assume a PC-compatible CMOS clock; invalid values leave
  `CLOCK_REALTIME` boot-relative as before.
- `CLOCK_REALTIME` advances with the monotonic timer and is not disciplined
  for drift after `clock_settime`, and there is no `adjtimex`.
- Timers are per-process (owner PID); delivery is process-directed, so a
  `SIGEV_THREAD_ID` target thread is not specifically selected.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Milestone 6: firmware and CPU substrate

This increment follows the shortest path toward one static Linux ELF. It adds the firmware handoff, frame allocator activation, paging primitives, privilege descriptors and fatal exception path.

## Implemented source paths

- `loader/main.c`: obtains and translates the final UEFI memory map, retries `ExitBootServices`, and transfers a versioned `BootInfo`.
- `tools/build-uefi.sh`: emits `build/efi/EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI` when passed the required COFF kernel objects/archive.
- `paging.rs`: creates four-level tables and changes individual 4 KiB PTEs with USER, WRITABLE, GLOBAL and NX bits.
- `descriptors.rs`: GDT, ring-0/user selectors, 64-bit TSS, RSP0 and IST1.
- `idt.rs` and `exceptions.S`: IDT installation and diagnostic handlers for default exceptions, double fault, and page fault.

## Integration blockers still open

The regular Rust target currently emits an ELF static archive. The UEFI PE/COFF link requires the kernel to be compiled as COFF objects or embedded as a separately loaded ELF image. `build-uefi.sh` intentionally accepts additional link inputs but does not claim that the existing ELF archive can be linked directly.

The page-table API also requires a platform `PhysAccess` implementation. It must expose page-table frames through a mapped physical-memory window. Activating a CR3 before the kernel image, stacks, BootInfo, framebuffer and physical window are all mapped would immediately fault, so activation is not performed during boot yet.

Exception handlers are currently fatal diagnostics. Recoverable usercopy faults require an exception-table mechanism and a distinction between user faults, kernel-copy faults and fatal kernel faults.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Milestone 60: POSIX record locks, OFD locks and robust-futex death handling

## Goal
Make `fcntl` record locking real: byte-range locks shared across processes
with `F_GETLK`/`F_SETLK`/`F_SETLKW`, their open-file-description variants
`F_OFD_*` with per-description ownership, and the exit-side half of the
robust-mutex protocol (walk the robust list, mark `FUTEX_OWNER_DIED`, wake
the waiter). Wiring the robust test exposed that `MAP_SHARED|MAP_ANONYMOUS`
pages were being COW-split at fork; shared anon pages now stay shared.

## Scope
- `fcntl(fd, F_GETLK/F_SETLK/F_SETLKW)` — POSIX record locks owned by the
  process, released when any descriptor of that inode closes in the process,
  at process exit, and when `F_SETLK`/`F_SETLKW` replaces the owner's own
  ranges.
- `fcntl(fd, F_OFD_GETLK/F_OFD_SETLK/F_OFD_SETLKW)` — locks owned by the
  open-file description: `dup`/`fork` share the owner, an independent
  `open(2)` contends in the same process, release happens at the last close.
- `set_robust_list` data is now acted on at exit: robust walks bounded,
  `FUTEX_OWNER_DIED` set preserving `FUTEX_WAITERS`, one waiter woken only
  when `FUTEX_WAITERS` was observed (Linux semantics), `list_op_pending`
  included, walk aborts on the first unreadable word.
- `MAP_SHARED|MAP_ANONYMOUS` areas stay physically shared (writable) across
  fork instead of being made COW.
- Blocking `F_SETLKW` returns `EINTR` for deliverable signals; `pause()`
  now shares that signal check.

Out of scope: deadlock detection (`F_SETLKW` never returns `EDEADLK`),
mandatory locking, `F_SETOWN`-style lock signals, `FUTEX_LOCK_PI`/priority
inheritance, real-time lock fairness ordering.

## Data model

### `record_lock.rs` — one table for both lock families
- Up to 64 entries `{ino, owner, exclusive, [start, end)}`; `owner` is
  `Pid(pid)` for POSIX locks or `Ofd(open-file id)` for `F_OFD_*`.
  `end == u64::MAX` represents end-of-file; `l_len == 0` maps to it.
- `F_SETLK` clears the owner's overlapping ranges first (trimming or
  splitting into two entries when the request lies strictly inside an
  existing one) and then places the new range; `l_len < 0` locks the
  backward range `[start+len, start)`.
- `F_GETLK` reports the first conflicting other-owner range with
  `l_whence = SEEK_SET` and the owner pid (0 for OFD locks, matching Linux)
  or `F_UNLCK` when no conflict exists.
- Non-blocking conflicts return `EAGAIN`; the table is fixed size and
  reports `ENOLCK` when full.
- One global wait queue serves `F_SETLKW`; any release wakes it and each
  waiter re-checks. A signal that becomes deliverable while waiting turns
  into `EINTR`.

### `fd.rs` — close semantics
- `release` (allocation unwind) stays silent, but real closes now go
  through `release_closed`: every close of an inode drops that process's
  POSIX locks on it (including implicit `CLOEXEC` closes at execve and
  `dup2` replacement), while OFD locks fall only when the description's
  last reference goes. `close_all` stays plain; `teardown_exit` calls
  `record_lock::release_pid` explicitly, so lock release does not depend
  on which context eventually drops the `Process`.

### `robust.rs` — exit-time protocol
- Walks `struct robust_list_head { list, futex_offset, list_op_pending }`
  in the exiting task's address space before teardown (the address space
  is still active), marks words whose low 30 bits equal the exiting pid
  with `OWNER_DIED`, and wakes one waiter only when `WAITERS` was set,
  exactly as Linux `handle_futex_death` does. The walk stops at the first
  unreadable word, so a corrupted list cannot loop or fault in kernel.

### `vm.rs` / `process.rs` — shared anonymous mappings
- `VmFlags::SHARED` marks `MAP_SHARED|MAP_ANON` areas at `mmap` time; after
  the fork clone makes every leaf read-only, the shared ranges are put
  back to writable on both sides, so the frames stay shared with no COW.

## Acceptance criteria
1. `./tools/acceptance-test.sh` prints `acceptance: PASS` with resource
   accounting intact.
2. `./tools/differential-test.sh` prints `differential test: PASS`,
   including new host/guest parity cases:
   - `DIFF_RECORDLOCK_OK`: cross-process `EAGAIN`, `F_GETLK` reporting
     (`l_start`/`l_len`/`l_pid`), `F_SETLKW` blocked until the owner
     unlocks, release on child exit, release on closing any descriptor.
   - `DIFF_OFDLOCK_OK`: two descriptions conflict in one process,
     `F_OFD_GETLK` reports owner pid 0, `dup` shares the owner, the lock
     survives closing one descriptor, dies at the last close.
   - `DIFF_ROBUSTFUTEX_OK`: a waiter with `FUTEX_WAITERS` armed is woken
     when the owner exits, the word carries `FUTEX_OWNER_DIED` with the
     TID bits cleared.
3. `./tools/check-source.sh` passes.

## Verification
- `cargo +nightly build -Zbuild-std=core --release` clean.
- Native (musl) smoke of `tests/differential.c` passes.
- `./tools/differential-test.sh` PASS, `./tools/acceptance-test.sh` PASS,
  `./tools/check-source.sh` pass.

## Risks
- Threads created with `clone` are modeled as separate tasks, so POSIX
  record locks are owned per task rather than per process; single-threaded
  musl usage (and the tests) is unaffected.
- Shared anonymous mappings only stay shared for pages that existed at
  fork time; a page first touched after fork diverges (no shared anon
  object/anon_vma equivalent yet).
- The lock table holds 64 entries (shared by both lock families); heavy
  lockers see `ENOLCK` rather than allocation.
- `F_SETLK` replaces the owner's overlapping ranges with no merge of
  adjacent same-type ranges, so many small requests can exhaust the table.
- Blocking waits use one global queue, so every release wakes all waiters
  for a re-check (bounded by the 8-task scheduler).
- No deadlock detection: a circular `F_SETLKW` chain sleeps instead of
  returning `EDEADLK`.
- Robust death handling covers the non-PI protocol only; `FUTEX_LOCK_PI`
  remains unsupported, so PI mutexes cannot be created in the first place.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
